Chern-Simons-Trinion Theories: One-form Symmetries and Superconformal Indices
Riccardo Comi, William Harding, and Noppadol Mekareeya

Abstract
We study 3d theories containing 𝒩 = 3 \mathcal{N}=3 Chern-Simons vector multiplets coupled to the SU ​ ( N ) 3 \mathrm{SU}(N)^{3} flavour symmetry of 3d T N T_{N} theories with Chern-Simons levels k 1 k_{1} , k 2 k_{2} and k 3 k_{3} . It was formerly pointed out that these theories flow to infrared SCFTs with enhanced 𝒩 = 4 \mathcal{N}=4 supersymmetry when 1 / k 1 + 1 / k 2 + 1 / k 3 = 0 1/k_{1}+1/k_{2}+1/k_{3}=0 . We examine superconformal indices of these theories which reveal that supersymmetry of the infrared SCFTs may get enhanced to 4 ≤ 𝒩 ≤ 6 4\leq\mathcal{N}\leq 6 if such a condition is satisfied. Moreover, even if the Chern-Simons levels do not obey the aforementioned condition, we find that there is still an infinite family of theories that flows to infrared SCFTs with 𝒩 = 4 \mathcal{N}=4 supersymmetry. The ’t Hooft anomalies of the one-form symmetries of these theories are analysed. As a by-product, we observe that there is generally a decoupled topological sector in the infrared. When the infrared SCFTs have 𝒩 ≥ 4 {\cal N}\geq 4 supersymmetry, we also study the Higgs and Coulomb branch limits of the indices which provide geometric information of the moduli space of the theories in question in terms of the Hilbert series.



1
Introduction

Three-dimensional (3d) supersymmetric Chern–Simons (CS)–matter theories have rich infrared (IR) behaviours. For example, the
{\cal N}=3
superconformal field theory (SCFT) can be obtained as the IR fixed point of the
{\cal N}=2
theory deformed by a certain superpotential
Gaiotto:2007qi
. Moreover, if the gauge algebra and matter content are chosen appropriately in an
{\cal N}=3
theory, supersymmetry may get further enhanced up to
{\cal N}=8

Gustavsson:2007vu
;
Bagger:2007jr
;
Bandres:2008vf
;
Gaiotto:2008sd
;
Hosomichi:2008jd
;
Hosomichi:2008jb
;
Aharony:2008ug
;
Schnabl:2008wj
;
Aharony:2008gk
. In particular, the
\U(N)_{k}\times\U(N)_{-k}
CS theory with two hypermultiplets in the bifundamental representation (in the 3d
{\cal N}=4
language) describes the system of
N
M2-branes on
\mathbb{C}^{4}/\mathbb{Z}_{k}
, where at large
N
it is dual to M-theory on
\mathrm{AdS}_{4}\times S^{7}/\mathbb{Z}_{k}
, and at large
N
with a fixed ratio
N/k
is dual to Type IIA string theory on
\mathrm{AdS}_{4}\times\mathbf{CP}^{3}
. This CS–matter theory provides the first explicit realisation of the AdS/CFT correspondence in these dimensions
Aharony:2008ug
.

In this article, we focus on 3d
{\cal N}=3
vector multiplets coupled to a certain number of copies of a 3d SCFT, known as the 3d
T_{N}
theory, whose flavour symmetry is
\mathrm{SU}(N)^{3}
. The 3d
T_{N}
theory can be realised by compactifying the 4d
T_{N}
theory
Gaiotto:2009we
;
Gaiotto:2009gz
on a circle, or equivalently by compactifying
N
M5-branes on a circle times a sphere with three full punctures. An example of the theories of our interest consists of that constructed from a single copy of the
T_{N}
theory such that the
\mathrm{SU}(N)^{3}
flavour symmetry is gauged with CS levels
k_{1}
,
k_{2}
and
k_{3}
. This can be realised by compactifying
N
M5-branes on a three-manifold which is a Seifert bundle over
S^{2}
with three singular fibres, with Seifert parameters
1/k_{1}
,
1/k_{2}
and
1/k_{3}

Eckhard:2019jgg
;
Assel:2022row
. This example can be generalised further, for example, by involving many copies of the
T_{N}
theories where each
\mathrm{SU}(N)
factor of the
\mathrm{SU}(N)^{3}
flavour symmetry of each copy is commonly gauged with CS levels
k_{1}
,
k_{2}
and
k_{3}
. The corresponding three-manifolds are then known as graph manifolds
Assel:2022row
. We remark
en passant
that, due to their richness in mathematical and physical properties, 3d theories arising from compactifying M5-branes on three-manifolds have received considerable attention over the recent years; see e.g.
Terashima:2011qi
;
Dimofte:2011ju
;
Gadde:2013sca
;
Chung:2014qpa
;
Gukov:2016gkn
;
Gukov:2017kmk
;
Gang:2018wek
;
Assel:2018vtq
;
Gang:2018huc
;
Cho:2020ljj
;
Gang:2021hrd
.

For the theories of our interest with general
N
, it was pointed out by the authors of
Assel:2022row
that whenever the CS levels satisfy the condition
\sum_{i=1}^{3}1/k_{i}=0
, then the theory flows to an IR SCFT with enhanced
{\cal N}=4
supersymmetry.
1
1

1

We remark that for
N=2
and a single copy of the
T_{2}
theory, the enhancement of supersymmetry to
{\cal N}=4
follows from the result of
Gaiotto:2008sd
.
For convenience, we shall refer to this condition as the Assel–Tachikawa–Tomasiello (ATT) condition. This statement was supported by a field theoretic argument. Geometrically, for a single copy of the
T_{N}
theory, supersymmetry enhancement is accounted for by the holonomy of the corresponding Seifert manifolds. However, when the theory contains more than one
T_{N}
building blocks, supersymmetry enhancement is left unaccounted for, in general, by the holonomy of graph manifolds.

One of the main objectives of this paper is to study supersymmetry enhancement of this family of theories, focusing on
N=2
and
N=3
, using the superconformal index
Bhattacharya:2008zy
;
Bhattacharya:2008bja
;
Kim:2009wb
;
Imamura:2011su
;
Kapustin:2011jm
;
Dimofte:2011py
;
Aharony:2013dha
;
Aharony:2013kma
.
2
2

2

We adopt the same notation as
Aharony:2013kma
;
Garozzo:2019ejm
;
Beratto:2021xmn
;
Mekareeya:2022spm
. Each notation is defined in the main text.
We find that, when the ATT condition is satisfied, supersymmetry of the IR SCFT generally gets enhanced to
{\cal N}=4
, but there are also a large number of cases with
{\cal N}=5
and
{\cal N}=6
supersymmetry. Surprisingly we also find that, even if the ATT condition is not satisfied, there is still an infinite family of theories whose IR SCFTs have enhanced
{\cal N}=4
supersymmetry. In particular, for certain special values of CS levels such as
(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})=(2,1,1)
, the IR SCFT turns out to be the rank-zero minimal
{\cal N}=4
SCFT, discussed in
Gang:2018huc
;
Gang:2021hrd
. Another main goal of this article is to study the one-form symmetries
Kapustin:2014gua
;
Gaiotto:2014kfa
of these theories, as well as their (mixed) ’t Hooft anomalies along the line of
Hsin:2018vcg
;
Eckhard:2019jgg
, and the mixed ’t Hooft anomalies between the one-form symmetries and zero-form symmetries using the method of
Mekareeya:2022spm
(see also
Sacchi:2023omn
). As a result, we deduce that there is generally a decoupled topological sector in the IR. We also identify these topological quantum field theories (TQFTs) using ’t Hooft anomalies of the one-form symmetries. Importantly, we point out that, even if a set of theories flow to the same IR SCFTs (which can be deduced from the fact that the indices are the same or the associated three-manifolds are diffeomorphic to each other), the decoupled topological sectors may be different. We also gauge the non-anomalous one-form symmetry and study the resulting theories. Finally, we study the Higgs and Coulomb limits of the superconformal indices
Razamat:2014pta
for theories whose IR SCFTs have
{\cal N}\geq 4
enhanced supersymmetry. These provide geometric information of the Higgs and Coulomb branches of the IR SCFTs in question in terms of the Hilbert series
Benvenuti:2006qr
;
Feng:2007ur
.

The paper is organised as follows. In Sections
2
and
3
, we study theories with one and two
T_{2}
building blocks, respectively. Their one-form symmetries and ’t Hooft anomalies are investigated in Section
2.1
. The theories whose CS levels satisfy the ATT condition are then studied in Sections
2.2
and
3.1
. In this class of theories, the Higgs and Coulomb branch limits of the indices are studied in Sections
2.2.3
and
3.1.3
. We then move on to explore theories whose CS levels do not satisfy the ATT condition in Sections
2.3
and
3.2
. We also study theories coupled to one or many copies of the
T(\mathrm{SU}(2))
SCFT in Sections
2.4
and
3.3
. In Section
4
, we discuss theories with
T_{3}
building blocks. We discuss their indices and ’t Hooft anomalies of one-form symmetries. Due to the technicality of the computations, we focus on the indices of theories whose CS levels satisfy the ATT condition, from which we conclude that the IR SCFT has enhanced
{\cal N}=4
supersymmetry. In Appendix
A
, we study certain theories with four
T_{2}
building blocks as well as their indices. In Appendix
B
, the mixed gauge/zero-form monopole operators in theories with one and two
T_{2}
building blocks such that the ATT condition is satisfied are examined. The potential mixed anomaly between the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
one-form symmetry and the zero-form flavour symmetry implied by the presence of such monopole operators is discussed.

Note added.

After this paper had appeared on the arXiv, we found
Li:2023ffx
with some overlapping content.

Theories with one
T_{2}
building block

T_{2}
theory whose three
\mathrm{SU}(2)
flavour symmetries are gauged with 3d
{\cal N}=3
Chern-Simons couplings
(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})
. We depict this theory diagrammatically by

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\end{split}

(2.1)

where each finite line with label
k_{i}
denotes an
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{k_{i}}
gauge group. This theory was studied extensively in
(
Assel:2022row
, Section 2.2.1)
, where it was pointed out that (
2.1
) can be realised by compactifying M5-branes on a three-manifold which is a Seifert bundle over
S^{2}
with three singular fibres, with Seifert parameters
1/k_{1}
,
1/k_{2}
and
1/k_{3}
.

The effective superpotential after integrating out the adjoint scalars is

\begin{split}W\propto\sum_{i=1}^{3}\frac{1}{k_{i}}\mathop{\rm tr}(\mu_{i}^{2})~,\end{split}

(2.2)

where
\mu_{i}
are the moment map operators for the
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{i}
symmetry of the
T_{2}
theory. In terms of the chiral fields
Q_{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}\alpha_{3}}
of the
T_{2}
theory,
\mu_{i}
can be written as

\begin{split}(\mu_{1})_{\alpha_{1}\alpha^{\prime}_{1}}&=\epsilon^{\alpha_{2}\alpha^{\prime}_{2}}\epsilon^{\alpha_{3}\alpha^{\prime}_{3}}Q_{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}\alpha_{3}}Q_{\alpha^{\prime}_{1}\alpha^{\prime}_{2}\alpha^{\prime}_{3}}~,\\
(\mu_{2})_{\alpha_{2}\alpha^{\prime}_{2}}&=\epsilon^{\alpha_{1}\alpha^{\prime}_{1}}\epsilon^{\alpha_{3}\alpha^{\prime}_{3}}Q_{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}\alpha_{3}}Q_{\alpha^{\prime}_{1}\alpha^{\prime}_{2}\alpha^{\prime}_{3}}~,\\
(\mu_{3})_{\alpha_{3}\alpha^{\prime}_{3}}&=\epsilon^{\alpha_{1}\alpha^{\prime}_{1}}\epsilon^{\alpha_{2}\alpha^{\prime}_{2}}Q_{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}\alpha_{3}}Q_{\alpha^{\prime}_{1}\alpha^{\prime}_{2}\alpha^{\prime}_{3}}~,\end{split}

(2.3)

where the indices
\alpha_{i},\alpha^{\prime}_{i}=1,2
correspond to the
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{i}
gauge group (with
i=1,2,3
). From the above relations, it follows that

\begin{split}\mathop{\rm tr}(\mu_{1}^{2})=\mathop{\rm tr}(\mu_{2}^{2})=\mathop{\rm tr}(\mu_{3}^{2})\equiv\mathop{\rm tr}(\mu^{2})\end{split}

(2.4)

and so the effective superpotential can be rewritten as

\begin{split}W\propto\left(\frac{1}{k_{1}}+\frac{1}{k_{2}}+\frac{1}{k_{3}}\right)\mathop{\rm tr}(\mu^{2})~.\end{split}

(2.5)

If the following condition is satisfied

\begin{split}\frac{1}{k_{1}}+\frac{1}{k_{2}}+\frac{1}{k_{3}}=0~,\end{split}

(2.6)

then supersymmetry gets enhanced from
{\cal N}=3
to
{\cal N}=4
. This follows from the discussion in
Gaiotto:2008sd
and also from
Schnabl:2008wj
;
Assel:2022row
. Relation (
2.6
) is what we referred to as the ATT condition in the introduction. Subsequently we will show that this is a
sufficient
, but
not
necessary, condition for supersymmetry enhancement. In particular, in Section
2.3
, we will show that even if the ATT condition (
2.6
) is not satisfied, there are cases in which the IR SCFT has accidental
{\cal N}=4
supersymmetry.

A main tool that we will use to analyse these theories is the superconformal index. It is explicitly given by

\begin{split}{\cal I}_{(\ref{singleT2})}(a,n_{a};x)&=\left(\frac{1}{8}\prod_{i=1}^{3}\oint\frac{dz_{i}}{2\pi iz_{i}}\right)\sum_{(m_{1},m_{2},m_{3})\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{3}z_{i}^{2k_{i}m_{i}}{\cal Z}^{\mathrm{SU}(2)}_{\text{vec}}(z_{i};m_{i};x)\right)\times\\
&\qquad\prod_{s_{1},s_{2},s_{3}=\pm 1}{\cal Z}^{1/2}_{\chi}(z_{1}^{s_{1}}z_{2}^{s_{2}}z_{3}^{s_{3}}a;s_{1}m_{1}+s_{2}m_{2}+s_{3}m_{3}+n_{a};x)~,\end{split}

(2.7)

where the
\mathrm{SU}(2)
vector multiplet contribution is

\begin{split}{\cal Z}^{\mathrm{SU}(2)}_{\text{vec}}(z;n;x)=x^{-2|n|}\prod_{s=\pm 1}(1-(-1)^{2n}x^{2|n|}z^{2s})~,\end{split}

(2.8)

and the contribution of the chiral multiplet of
R
-charge
R
is

\begin{split}{\cal Z}^{R}_{\chi}(z;m;x)=\left(x^{1-R}z^{-1}\right)^{|m|/2}\prod_{j=0}^{\infty}\frac{1-(-1)^{m}z^{-1}x^{|m|+2-R+2j}}{1-(-1)^{m}z\,x^{|m|+R+2j}}~.\end{split}

(2.9)

If the CS levels satisfy the ATT condition (
2.6
), it follows from (
2.5
) that the effective superpotential is zero, and the
\U(1)_{a}
flavour symmetry which assigns charge
+1
to all of the eight chiral multiplets of the
T_{2}
theory is a symmetry of the Lagrangian. We denote by
a
and
n_{a}
the fugacity and background magnetic flux for this flavour symmetry. Upon computing the series expansion of the index, we will set
n_{a}=0
and drop the
n_{a}
dependence from the index, i.e. we write the latter simply as
{\cal I}_{(\ref{singleT2})}(a;x)
. Note also that, if the CS levels do not satisfy the ATT condition (
2.6
), we should set
a=1
and
n_{a}=0
in (
2.7
), since the
\U(1)_{a}
flavour symmetry is no longer a symmetry of the effective superpotential (
2.5
).

2.1
One-form symmetries and their ’t Hooft anomalies

We now discuss the one-form symmetries of theory (
2.1
) and their anomalies. Let us first consider the
T_{2}
theory, whose global form of the manifest flavour symmetry is (see
(
Bhardwaj:2021ojs
, (4.2))
)

\begin{split}\frac{\mathrm{SU}(2)_{1}\times\mathrm{SU}(2)_{2}\times\mathrm{SU}(2)_{3}}{(\mathbb{Z}_{2})_{13}\times(\mathbb{Z}_{2})_{23}}~,\end{split}

(2.10)

where
(\mathbb{Z}_{2})_{ij}
denotes the diagonal
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
subgroup of the centre of
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{i}
times the centre of
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{j}
. In other words, among the three
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
factors that come from the centre of
\prod_{i=1}^{3}\mathrm{SU}(2)_{i}
in the numerator of (
2.10
), only two combinations present in the denominator act trivially on the four free hypermultiplets of the
T_{2}
theory. Gauging each of the
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{i}
symmetry therefore leads to the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}
one-form symmetry. We will see that turning on CS levels
k_{i}
for each
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{i}
gauge group (with
i=1,2,3
) results in ’t Hooft anomalies of a subgroup or the whole
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{2}
one-form symmetry.

The ’t Hooft anomaly of the one-form symmetry in theory (
2.1
) with CS levels
(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})
is characterised by the 4d anomaly theory whose action is
Benini:2017dus

\begin{split}\frac{2\pi}{2}\int_{{\cal M}_{4}}\sum_{i=1}^{3}k_{i}\frac{{\cal P}(w_{i}^{(2)})}{2}~,\end{split}

(2.11)

where each of
w_{i}^{(2)}\in H^{2}({\cal M}_{4},\mathbb{Z}_{2})
is the two-form background field for each
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
one-form symmetry that arises from the centre of each
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{i}
gauge group,
{\cal P}(w^{(2)})
is the Pontryagin square operation and the integration is performed over a spin manifold
{\cal M}_{4}
. Note that
\int_{{\cal M}_{4}}{\cal P}(w_{i}^{(2)})
is even on a spin manifold
{\cal M}_{4}
.

Since among the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{3}
centres of
\mathrm{SU}(2)^{3}
only
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{2}
acts non-trivially on the trifundamental matter of the
T_{2}
theory, we have the condition

\begin{split}\sum_{i=1}^{3}w_{i}^{(2)}=0~.\end{split}

(2.12)

Using the identity

\begin{split}\int_{{\cal M}_{4}}{\cal P}(A+B)&=\int_{{\cal M}_{4}}{\cal P}(A)+\int_{{\cal M}_{4}}{\cal P}(B)+2\int_{{\cal M}_{4}}A\cup B~,\\
\end{split}

(2.13)

and the fact that
\int_{{\cal M}_{4}}{\cal P}(w_{i}^{(2)})
is even on spin manifold
{\cal M}_{4}
, we rewrite (
2.11
) as

\begin{split}S_{\text{anom}}=\frac{2\pi}{2}\int_{{\cal M}_{4}}\left[(k_{1}+k_{3})\frac{{\cal P}(w_{1}^{(2)})}{2}+(k_{2}+k_{3})\frac{{\cal P}(w_{2}^{(2)})}{2}-k_{3}(w_{1}^{(2)}\cup w_{2}^{(2)})\right]\end{split}

(2.14)

after dropping terms that are integer multiples of
2\pi
. Using the notation of
(
Hsin:2018vcg
, (F.8))

\begin{split}S_{\text{anom}}=\frac{2\pi}{2}\left[\sum_{I=1}^{2}p_{II}\int_{{\cal M}_{4}}\frac{{\cal P}(w_{I}^{(2)})}{2}+p_{12}\int_{{\cal M}_{4}}w_{1}^{(2)}\cup w_{2}^{(2)}\right]~,\end{split}

(2.15)

we see that the above anomalies can be summarised in following symmetric matrix
p
:

\begin{split}p=\begin{pmatrix}k_{1}+k_{3}\quad&\quad-k_{3}\\
-k_{3}\quad&\quad k_{2}+k_{3}\end{pmatrix}\quad{\rm mod}~2~.\end{split}

(2.16)

For
k_{3}=1
, this is in agreement with the anomaly matrix given by
(
Eckhard:2019jgg
, (4.58))
with
N=n=2
and
k_{1,2}\rightarrow k_{1,2}+1
.

We can decompose the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{2}
one-form symmetry of (
2.1
) into two parts: a subgroup
\Gamma_{A}
of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{2}
that has an ’t Hooft anomaly and the ’t Hooft anomaly free part
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{2}/\Gamma_{A}
. The subgroup
\Gamma_{A}
is given by
\Gamma_{A}=\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{\mathrm{rank}(p)}
, where
\mathrm{rank}(p)
is the rank of matrix
p
. The ’t Hooft anomaly free part is then
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{\dim(\mathrm{ker}\,p)}
, where
\mathrm{ker}\,p
denotes the kernel (nullspace) of matrix
p
.

According to
(
Eckhard:2019jgg
, (4.44))
, it was proposed that theory (
2.1
) flows to an IR theory that splits into two subsectors:

1.

the minimal abelian TQFT
Hsin:2018vcg
:

\begin{split}{\cal A}^{\Gamma_{A},p}=\begin{cases}{\cal A}^{2,1}\cong\mathrm{SU}(2)_{1}\cong\mathrm{U}(1)_{2}\quad&\text{if $\mathrm{rank}(p)=1$}\\
\text{${\cal A}^{\{2,2\},p}$ of \cite[cite]{(\@@bibref{AuthorsPhrase1Year}{Hsin:2018vcg}{\@@citephrase{, }}{}, Appendix F)}}\quad&\text{if $\mathrm{rank}(p)=2$}\end{cases}~;\end{split}

(2.17)

2.

the subsector
(\ref{singleT2})_{\text{AF}}
, which can be an interacting SCFT or another TQFT, whose one-form symmetry is ’t Hooft anomaly free.

The above statement can be summarised as
3
3

3

A piece of evidence that supports this statement is as follows. We observe that there exist theories associated with diffeomorphic three-manifolds (their superconformal indices are equal) whose anomaly matrices
p
are inequivalent; see Footnote
13
. For such theories, we interpret this phenomenon in the following way. The subsectors
(\ref{singleT2})_{\text{AF}}
, characterised by the index, are the same. However, the topological sectors, characterised by the anomaly matrices, are different.

\begin{split}(\ref{singleT2})=(\ref{singleT2})_{\text{AF}}\otimes{\cal A}^{\Gamma_{A},p}~.\end{split}

(2.18)

This relation can be inverted as in
(
Hsin:2018vcg
, (1.13))
and
(
Eckhard:2019jgg
, (4.45))
, namely

\begin{split}(\ref{singleT2})_{\text{AF}}=\frac{(\ref{singleT2})\otimes{\cal A}^{\Gamma_{A},-p}}{\Gamma_{A}}~.\end{split}

(2.19)

2.2
Cases that satisfy the ATT condition

2.6
).

(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})=(-k,2k,2k)

(2.20)

can also be regarded as the

gauge theory with a bifundamental half-hypermultiplet in the representation
[\mathbf{2};\mathbf{4}]
whose quiver diagram is

(2.21)

The equivalence of these two theories is due to the fact that
\mathrm{Spin}(4)\cong\mathrm{SU}(2)\times\mathrm{SU}(2)
and that the vector representation
[\mathbf{4}]
of
\mathrm{Spin}(4)
is equivalent to the representation
[\mathbf{2};\mathbf{2}]
of
\mathrm{SU}(2)\times\mathrm{SU}(2)
. The index of this theory can be derived as in
Aharony:2013kma
;
Beratto:2021xmn
. We first compute the index for the
\USp(2)_{-k}\times\mathrm{SO}(4)_{2k}
gauge theory with the same matter content, namely

\begin{split}\hbox to143.88pt{\vbox to62.11pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 73.73167pt\lower-31.05254pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -16.36 0 C -16.36 23.58 -35.48 42.69 -59.06 42.69 C -82.63 42.69 -101.75 23.58 -101.75 0 C -101.75 -23.58 -82.63 -42.69 -59.06 -42.69 C -35.48 -42.69 -16.36 -23.58 -16.36 0 Z M -59.06 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-69.23454pt}{-2.41666pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{65}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 96.79 0 C 96.79 20.84 79.89 37.73 59.06 37.73 C 38.22 37.73 21.32 20.84 21.32 0 C 21.32 -20.84 38.22 -37.73 59.06 -37.73 C 79.89 -37.73 96.79 -20.84 96.79 0 Z M 59.06 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{19.70244pt}{-2.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{65}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{}}{}{{}}
{{{{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}}}}{}{{{{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}}}}{{}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setdash{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -16.09 0 L 21.05 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\end{split}

(2.22)

The index of this theory is

\begin{split}&{\cal I}_{(\ref{USp2SO4})}(\zeta,a;x)\\
&=\frac{1}{8}\sum_{(\mathfrak{m}_{1},\mathfrak{m}_{2})\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\,\sum_{\mathfrak{n}\in\mathbb{Z}}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{2}\oint\frac{dv_{j}}{2\pi iv_{j}}\,v_{j}^{2k\mathfrak{m}_{j}}\right)\zeta^{\mathfrak{m}_{1}+\mathfrak{m}_{2}}\oint\frac{du}{2\pi iu}\,u^{-2kn}\times\\
&\quad{\cal Z}^{\mathrm{SO}(4)}_{\text{vec}}(v_{1},v_{2};\mathfrak{m}_{1},\mathfrak{m}_{2};x){\cal Z}^{\USp(2)}_{\text{vec}}(u;\mathfrak{n};x)\times\\
&\quad\prod_{i=1}^{2}\,\prod_{s_{1},s_{2}=\pm 1}{\cal Z}^{1/2}_{\chi}\left(v_{i}^{s_{1}}u^{s_{2}}a;s_{1}\mathfrak{m}_{i}+{s_{2}}\mathfrak{n};x\right)~,\end{split}

(2.23)

where
\zeta
is the fugacity for the zero-form magnetic symmetry such that
\zeta^{2}=1
, and the contribution of the
\mathrm{SO}(4)
vector multiplet is

\begin{split}{\cal Z}^{\mathrm{SO}(4)}_{\text{vec}}(v_{1},v_{2};\mathfrak{m}_{1},\mathfrak{m}_{2};x)&=x^{-|\mathfrak{m}_{1}-\mathfrak{m}_{2}|-|\mathfrak{m}_{1}+\mathfrak{m}_{2}|}\times\\
&\qquad\prod_{s_{1},s_{2}=\pm 1}\left(1-(-1)^{s_{1}\mathfrak{m}_{1}+s_{2}\mathfrak{m}_{2}}x^{|s_{1}\mathfrak{m}_{1}+s_{2}\mathfrak{m}_{2}|}v_{1}^{s_{1}}v_{2}^{s_{2}}\right)~.\end{split}

(2.24)

For simplicity, we have set the fugacity
\chi
for the charge conjugation symmetry to unity. Theory (
2.21
) can be obtained by gauging the magnetic symmetry; its index reads

\begin{split}{\cal I}_{(\ref{USp2Spin4})}(a;x)=\frac{1}{2}\left[{\cal I}_{(\ref{USp2SO4})}(\zeta=1,a;x)+{\cal I}_{(\ref{USp2SO4})}(\zeta=-1,a;x)\right]~.\end{split}

(2.25)

It can be checked that (
2.7
) and (
2.25
) yield the same result for
(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})=(-k,2k,2k)
. In fact, the gauge fugacities and magnetic fluxes in (
2.7
) and (
2.23
) can be mapped to each other as follows:

\begin{split}\begin{array}[]{lll}{z_{1}}={u}~,&\qquad{z_{2}^{2}}={v_{1}}{v_{2}}~,&\qquad{z_{3}^{2}}={v_{1}}{v_{2}^{-1}}~,\\
{m_{1}}={\mathfrak{n}}~,&\qquad{2}{m_{2}}={\mathfrak{m}_{1}}+{\mathfrak{m}_{2}}~,&\qquad{2}{m_{3}}={\mathfrak{m}_{1}}-{\mathfrak{m}_{2}}~.\end{array}\end{split}

(2.26)

The indices up to order
x^{4}
are as follows.

\begin{split}\begin{tabular}[]{|c|c|}\hline\cr$k$&Index\\
\hline\cr$1$, $2$&diverges\\
$3$&$1+0x+(2a^{4}-1)x^{2}+\left(a^{6}-a^{2}+a^{-2}\right)x^{3}+\left(3a^{8}-a^{4}-2\right)x^{4}+\ldots$\\
$4$&$1+0x+(a^{4}-1)x^{2}+a^{-2}x^{3}+(2a^{8}-2)x^{4}+\ldots$\\
$\geq\,5$&$1+0x+(a^{4}-1)x^{2}+a^{-2}x^{3}+(a^{8}-2)x^{4}+\ldots$\\
\hline\cr\end{tabular}\end{split}

(2.27)

Note that, for
k\geq 5
, the indices for these cases differ from each other at a higher order than
x^{4}
. For
k=1,\,2
, the index diverges and the theories are
bad
in the sense of Gaiotto and Witten
Gaiotto:2008ak
.

Let us now analyse the superconformal multiplets and enhancement of supersymmetry for the cases of
k\geq 3
using information from
Cordova:2016emh
;
Razamat:2016gzx
;
Evtikhiev:2017heo
(see also the argument in
Garozzo:2019ejm
;
Beratto:2020qyk
). The vanishing coefficient of
x
implies that there is no 3d
{\cal N}=3
flavour current multiplet
B_{1}[0]_{1}^{(2)}
. In general, the negative terms at order
x^{2}
receive the contribution from the
{\cal N}=3
flavour current multiplet
B_{1}[0]_{1}^{(2)}
and
{\cal N}=3
extra-SUSY current multiplet
A_{2}[0]_{1}^{(0)}
. Since the former is absent and the only negative term at order
x^{2}
is
-1
, we conclude that there is precisely one
{\cal N}=3
extra SUSY-current multiplet that leads to the enhanced
{\cal N}=4
supersymmetry in the IR.

As can be read off from the index, the bare monopole operator with magnetic fluxes
(m_{1},m_{2},m_{3})
has dimension

\begin{split}\Delta(m_{1},m_{2},m_{3})=\frac{1}{4}\sum_{s_{1},s_{2},s_{3}=\pm 1}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{3}s_{i}m_{i}\right|-\sum_{i=1}^{3}2|m_{i}|~,\end{split}

(2.28)

its charge under the
\U(1)_{a}
flavour symmetry is

\begin{split}a(m_{1},m_{2},m_{3})=-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{s_{1},s_{2},s_{3}=\pm 1}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{3}s_{i}m_{i}\right|\end{split}

(2.29)

and it carries charge
2k_{i}m_{i}
under the Cartan subalgebra of each
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{i}
gauge factor.

Viewing the theory in question from the 3d
{\cal N}=2
perspective, the
\U(1)_{a}
flavour current multiplet also contributes
-1
at order
x^{2}
in the index. As a consequence the aforementioned
{\cal N}=3
extra SUSY-current should be identified with that of the flavour symmetry. Furthermore, we see that there is no relevant operator due to the vanishing coefficient of
x
. For
k\geq 4
, there is one marginal operator corresponding to
\mathop{\rm tr}(\mu_{1}^{2})=\mathop{\rm tr}(\mu_{2}^{2})=\mathop{\rm tr}(\mu_{3}^{2})
, where
\mu_{i}
is the moment map of the
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{i}
flavour symmetry of the
T_{2}
theory. For
k=3
, we have an extra marginal operator which, according to (
2.7
), receives the contribution from the eight gauge magnetic fluxes
(m_{1},m_{2},m_{3})=(\pm 2,\pm 1,\pm 1)
, where
\pm
here denotes all possible 8 sign combinations that can appear. Since the bare (non-gauge-invariant) monopole operators with such fluxes contribute
z_{1}^{\mp 12}z_{2}^{\pm 12}z_{3}^{\pm 12}
,
x^{-4}
and
a^{-8}
to the index, we interpret the aforementioned marginal operator as a dressed monopole operator, where such a bare monopole operator is dressed in a gauge invariant way with a combination of
12
chiral fields of the
T_{2}
theory.

One-form symmetries and gauging thereof

We now examine the one-form symmetry of (
2.1
) with
(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})=(-k,2k,2k)
. From the discussion in Section
2.1
, we see that

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.8}{
\begin{tabular}[]{|c|c|c|c|c|}\hline\cr CS levels&Anomaly&Non-anomalous&Anomalous&TQFT with\\
$(-k,2k,2k)$&matrix $p$&1-form symmetry&1-form symmetry&anom. symmetry\\
\hline\cr$k$ even&$\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}$&$\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{2}$&$\mathbf{1}$&$-$\\
\hline\cr$k$ odd&$\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}$&$\mathbb{Z}_{2}$&$\mathbb{Z}_{2}$&${\cal A}^{2,1}\cong\mathrm{SU}(2)_{1}\cong\U(1)_{2}$\\
\hline\cr\end{tabular}}\end{split}

(2.30)

For
k
even, theory (
2.20
) flows to an
{\cal N}=4
SCFT with a non-anomalous
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{2}
one-form symmetry. However, for
k
odd, theory (
2.20
) flows to an
{\cal N}=4
SCFT with a non-anomalous
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
one-form symmetry and a decoupled topological sector
{\cal A}^{2,1}
.

We can understand the above statement from another point of view. A slight modification of
(
Tachikawa:2019dvq
, (3.27))
(see also
(
Tachikawa:2019dvq
, (3.18), (3.19))
) states that the
\USp(2N)_{-k}\times\mathrm{SO}(2M)_{2k}
gauge theory with bifundamental matter admits a quotient by a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry, whose generator is a combination of the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
centres of
\mathrm{SO}(2M)
and
\USp(2N)
, if
4
4

4

More generally, for the
\USp(2N)_{k_{2}}\times\mathrm{SO}(2M)_{k_{1}}
gauge theory with bifundamental matter, this condition reads
\frac{1}{4}k_{1}M+\frac{1}{2}k_{2}N\in\mathbb{Z}
.

\begin{split}\frac{1}{2}k(M-N)\in\mathbb{Z}~.\end{split}

(2.31)

In other words, the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
one-form symmetry of the
\USp(2N)_{-k}\times\mathrm{SO}(2M)_{2k}
gauge theory is non-anomalous if (
2.31
) is satisfied. Applying this to (
2.22
), namely
M=2
and
N=1
, we see that its non-anomalous one-form symmetry is

\begin{split}\text{one-form symmetry of}~(\ref{USp2SO4})=\begin{cases}\mathbb{Z}_{2}&\quad\text{$k$ even}\\
\text{trivial}&\quad\text{$k$ odd}\end{cases}~.\end{split}

(2.32)

Recall that theory (
2.21
), or equivalently theory (
2.20
), arises from gauging the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
zero-form magnetic symmetry of (
2.22
). Since gauging a discrete
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
zero-form symmetry in 3d leads to a dual
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
one-form symmetry, we conclude that for
k
odd theory (
2.21
) = (
2.20
) has a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
one-form symmetry. For
k
even, the one-form symmetry of theory (
2.21
) = (
2.20
) can be either
\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}
or its extension
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
. Note that the extension is formed if theory (
2.22
), with
k
even, has a mixed anomaly between the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
zero-form magnetic symmetry and the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
one-form symmetry
Tachikawa:2017gyf
. Subsequently, we will explicitly show that there is no extension of the symmetry to
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
.

For this purpose, let us gauge the
whole
one-form symmetry of theory (
2.21
), i.e. we turn it into a dual zero-form symmetry. This is equivalent to gauging the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
one-form symmetry in (
2.22
), namely considering the
[\USp(2)_{-k}\times\mathrm{SO}(4)_{2k}]/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
theory. The index of the latter can be computed as in (
2.23
) but with the inclusion of the summation over half-odd-integral fluxes; in particular, we modify the summation in (
2.23
) as follows:

\begin{split}\sum_{(\mathfrak{m}_{1},\mathfrak{m}_{2})\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\,\sum_{\mathfrak{n}\in\mathbb{Z}}\quad\longrightarrow\quad\sum_{p^{\prime}=0}^{1}s^{p^{\prime}}\sum_{(\mathfrak{m}_{1},\mathfrak{m}_{2})\in\left(\mathbb{Z}+\frac{p^{\prime}}{2}\right)^{2}}\,\sum_{\mathfrak{n}\in\mathbb{Z}+\frac{p^{\prime}}{2}}~,\end{split}

(2.33)

where
s
is a fugacity for the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
zero-form symmetry arising from gauging the one-form symmetry such that
s^{2}=1
. For
k
odd, we see that the half-odd-integral fluxes (i.e.those correspond to
p^{\prime}=1
) do not contribute to the index; in other words, the index of the
[\USp(2)_{-k}\times\mathrm{SO}(4)_{2k}]/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
theory is equal to that of the
\USp(2)_{-k}\times\mathrm{SO}(4)_{2k}
theory. This is in agreement with the proposal that the one-form symmetry of (
2.22
) for
k
odd has a non-trivial ’t Hooft anomaly.
5
5

5

One can also check, in the same way as in
Mekareeya:2022spm
;
Sacchi:2023omn
, that for
k
odd the integrand of the index (
2.23
) contains fractional powers of
\mathrm{SO}(4)
gauge fugacities for the magnetic fluxes
(\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{m}_{1},\mathfrak{m}_{2})=(1/2,1/2,1/2)
.
Let us focus on
k
even. We see that for
p^{\prime}=0
the contribution from
\zeta^{\mathfrak{m}_{1}+\mathfrak{m}_{2}}
is either 1 or
\zeta
, whereas for
p^{\prime}=1
we have either
s
or
s\zeta
. The elements of
\{1,\zeta,s,s\zeta\}
form the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}
zero-from symmetry. Observe that the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
zero-form symmetry associated with
s
and the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
zero-form magnetic symmetry associated with
\zeta
do not form an extension to
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
, since there is no element of order
4
.

We can also see this from the perspective of theory (
2.20
). In order to gauge the whole one-form symmetry, we can do it in two steps. First, gauge the diagonal
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
one-form symmetry, whose generator is a combination of the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
centres of
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{2k}
and
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{2k}
; in other words, we consider the quotient
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{-k}\times[\mathrm{SU}(2)_{2k}\times\mathrm{SU}(2)_{2k}]/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
. The latter is equivalent to (
2.22
), namely the
\USp(2)_{-k}\times\mathrm{SO}(4)_{2k}
gauge theory, since
\mathrm{SO}(4)\cong[\mathrm{SU}(2)\times\mathrm{SU}(2)]/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
. We report the indices up to order
x^{4}
below.

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.95}{
\begin{tabular}[]{|c|c|}\hline\cr$k$&Index\\
\hline\cr$1$, $2$&diverges\\
\hline\cr$3$&$1+\zeta a^{2}x+\left[(2+\zeta)a^{4}-(1+\zeta)\right]x^{2}+\left[(1+2\zeta)a^{6}-(1+\zeta)a^{2}+a^{-2}\right]x^{3}+$\\
&$\left[(3+2\zeta)a^{8}-(1+\zeta)a^{4}-2\right]x^{4}+\ldots$\\
\hline\cr$4$&$1+0x+\left[(1+\zeta)a^{4}-1\right]x^{2}+\left[\zeta(a^{6}-a^{2})+a^{-2}\right]x^{3}+$\\
&$\left[(2+\zeta)a^{8}-\zeta a^{4}-2\right]x^{4}+\ldots$\\
\hline\cr$5$&$1+0x+\left(a^{4}-1\right)x^{2}+\left(\zeta a^{6}+a^{-2}\right)x^{3}+\left[(1+\zeta)a^{8}-\zeta a^{4}-2\right]x^{4}+\ldots$\\
\hline\cr$\geq\,6$&$1+0x+(a^{4}-1)x^{2}+a^{-2}x^{3}+\left[(1+\zeta)a^{8}-2\right]x^{4}+\ldots$\\
\hline\cr\end{tabular}}\end{split}

(2.34)

For
k\geq 6
, the terms with fugacity
\zeta
appear at a higher order than
x^{4}
.

The second step is to gauge the remaining of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
one-form symmetry, i.e. we consider the further quotient
[\mathrm{SU}(2)_{-k}\times[\mathrm{SU}(2)_{2k}\times\mathrm{SU}(2)_{2k}]/\mathbb{Z}_{2}]/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
. The index of the latter can be obtained from (
2.7
) by replacing the summation as

\begin{split}\sum_{(m_{1},m_{2},m_{3})\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\quad\longrightarrow\quad\sum_{p^{\prime}=0}^{1}s^{p^{\prime}}\sum_{m_{1}\in\left(\mathbb{Z}+\frac{p^{\prime}}{2}\right)}\,\,\,\sum_{p=0}^{1}\zeta^{p}\sum_{(m_{2},m_{3})\in\left(\mathbb{Z}+\frac{p}{2}\right)\times\left(\mathbb{Z}+\frac{p}{2}+\frac{p^{\prime}}{2}\right)}~,\end{split}

(2.35)

where
\zeta
is the fugacity of the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
zero-form symmetry arising from the first step and
s
is that arising from the second step.
6
6

6

Observe that we have four mutually exclusive cases, namely (1)
p=0
and
p^{\prime}=0
:
m_{1},m_{2},m_{3}
are integral and we have
\zeta^{0}s^{0}=1
; (2)
p=1
and
p^{\prime}=0
:
m_{1}
is integral,
m_{2},m_{3}
are half-odd-integral and we have
\zeta^{1}s^{0}=\zeta
; (3)
p=0
and
p^{\prime}=1
:
m_{1}
is half-odd-integral,
m_{2}
is integral,
m_{3}
is half-odd-integral and we have
\zeta^{0}s^{1}=s
; (4)
p=1
and
p^{\prime}=1
:
m_{1}
is half-odd-integral,
m_{2}
is half-odd-integral,
m_{3}
is integral and we have
\zeta s
.
We deliberately used the same notation as those for the
[\USp(2)_{-k}\times\mathrm{SO}(4)_{2k}]/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
theory since they can be identified with each other. As before, the elements of
\{1,\zeta,s,s\zeta\}
form the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}
zero-from symmetry. Moreover, it is clear from (
2.35
) that the order of gauging of the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
one-form symmetry in each of the two steps is immaterial; this confirms that the one-form symmetry of (
2.20
) is indeed
\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}
for
k
even.

For reference, we report the index for the
[\USp(2)_{-k}\times\mathrm{SO}(4)_{2k}]/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
theory or the
[\mathrm{SU}(2)_{-k}\times[\mathrm{SU}(2)_{2k}\times\mathrm{SU}(2)_{2k}]/\mathbb{Z}_{2}]/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
theory, with
k=4
, up to order
x^{9}
as follows:

\begin{split}1+&(-1+a^{4}+a^{4}\zeta)x^{2}+(a^{-2}-a^{2}\zeta+a^{6}\zeta)x^{3}+(-2+2a^{8}-a^{4}\zeta+a^{8}\zeta)x^{4}+\\
&(-a^{6}+a^{10}+a^{10}\zeta)x^{5}+(1+a^{-4}-a^{4}-a^{8}+2a^{12}-2a^{4}\zeta+2a^{12}\zeta)x^{6}+\\
&(-2a^{-2}-a^{6}+a^{14}+2a^{2}\zeta-2a^{6}\zeta-a^{10}\zeta+2a^{14}\zeta)x^{7}+\\
&(-a^{4}-2a^{8}+3a^{16}-s+a^{4}s-\zeta+a^{4}\zeta-a^{12}\zeta+2a^{16}\zeta-s\zeta+a^{4}s\zeta)x^{8}+\\
&(a^{-6}+4a^{2}+2a^{6}-a^{10}-a^{14}+2a^{18}+\\
&\ 2a^{-2}s-2a^{2}s-5a^{6}\zeta-a^{10}\zeta+2a^{18}\zeta+2a^{-2}s\zeta-2a^{2}s\zeta)x^{9}+\ldots~.\end{split}

(2.36)

On the other hand, for
k
odd, we find that the magnetic fluxes in the sector
p^{\prime}=1
contribute zero to the index, and so the fugacity
s
does not appear.

Higgs and Coulomb branches

Let us now explore the Higgs and Coulomb branches of the IR 3d
{\cal N}=4
SCFT associated with this class of theories. We can take the Higgs and Coulomb branch limit of the index in a similar way as in
Razamat:2014pta
to obtain the Higgs and Coulomb branch Hilbert series as follows. We define

\begin{split}&h=xa^{2}~,~c=xa^{-2}~,\\
&\text{or equivalently}\quad x=(hc)^{1/2}~,~a=(h/c)^{1/4}\end{split}

(2.37)

and substitute them in the index (
2.7
). In the Higgs branch limit we send
c\rightarrow 0
and keep
h
fixed, whereas in the Coulomb branch limit we send
h\rightarrow 0
and keep
c
fixed.

Let us now apply this to (
2.1
) with CS levels
(-k,2k,2k)
. We obtain the Higgs and Coulomb branch limits of (
2.7
) to be

\begin{split}\text{Higgs limit $(\ref{singleT2})_{(-k,2k,2k)}$:}&\qquad\mathop{\rm PE}\left[h^{2}+h^{2k-4}+h^{2k-3}-h^{4k-6}\right]~,\\
\text{Coulomb limit $(\ref{singleT2})_{(-k,2k,2k)}$:}&\qquad 1~.\end{split}

(2.38)

The former is indeed the Hilbert series of
\mathbb{C}^{2}/\widehat{D}_{2k-2}

(
Benvenuti:2006qr
, (5.31))
. These indicate that Higgs and Coulomb branches of the IR
{\cal N}=4
interacting SCFT associated with (
2.1
) with CS levels
{(-k,2k,2k)}
are
\mathbb{C}^{2}/\widehat{D}_{2k-2}
and trivial, respectively. The generators of the Higgs branch, corresponding to
h^{2}
,
h^{2k-4}
and
h^{2k-3}
, are respectively

\begin{split}w=\mathop{\rm tr}(\mu^{2})~,\quad v=X_{(2,1,1)}Q^{4k}~,\quad u=X_{(2,1,1)}Q^{4k-4}\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{3}~,\end{split}

(2.39)

satisfying the relation

\begin{split}u^{2}+v^{2}w=w^{2k-3}~.\end{split}

(2.40)

Here
X_{(2,1,1)}
denotes the bare monopole operator of flux
(2,1,1)
, where it has dimension
-4
and carries the flavour charge
-8
as well as gauge charges
(-4k,4k,4k)
under the Cartan subalgebras of each
SU(2)_{i}
; see around (
2.28
). In the above
v=X_{(2,1,1)}Q^{4k}
denotes the gauge invariant dressed monopole operator, where the bare monopoles with fluxes
(\pm 2,\pm 1,\pm 1)
are dressed with appropriate combinations of
4k
chiral multiplets
Q
of the
T_{2}
theory. Note that
X_{(2,1,1)}
contains
4k
gauge indices of each
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{i}
and these are contracted with the gauge indices in
Q^{4k}
to form
v
. Similarly, for
u
, the gauge indices of
X_{(2,1,1)}
are contracted with those of
Q^{4k-4}
as well as
\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{3}
, and the remaining indices are contracted with epsilon tensors.

Let us revisit the special case of
k=2
, i.e. the CS levels
(-2,4,4)
. Although the index diverges, the above computation shows that the Higgs branch is
\mathbb{C}^{2}/\widehat{D}_{2}
. This is reminiscent of the Higgs branch of the 3d
{\cal N}=4

\mathrm{SU}(2)
gauge theory with
2
hypermultiplets in the fundamental representation. The latter is the union of two isomorphic hyperKähler cones, each described by
\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}

Seiberg:1996nz
;
Gaiotto:2008ak
;
Ferlito:2016grh
;
Assel:2018exy
. We believe that the Higgs branch of the case of
k=2
has the same structure.

We can also study of the Higgs and Coulomb branch limits of the
\USp(2)_{-k}\times\mathrm{SO}(4)_{2k}
gauge theory, or equivalently
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{-k}\times[\mathrm{SU}(2)_{2k}\times\mathrm{SU}(2)_{2k}]/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
, which comes from gauging a non-anomalous
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
subgroup of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{2}
one-form symmetry of the aforementioned theory. We find that the Higgs and Coulomb branch limits are
7
7

7

Throughout the paper, we put superscript
[1]
whenever we would like to emphasise that the corresponding symmetry is one-form.

\begin{split}\text{Higgs limit $(\ref{singleT2})_{(-k,2k,2k)}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[1]}$:}&\qquad\mathop{\rm PE}\left[h^{2}+h^{k-2}+h^{k-1}-h^{2k-2}\right]~,\\
\text{Coulomb limit $(\ref{singleT2})_{(-k,2k,2k)}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[1]}$:}&\qquad 1~.\end{split}

(2.41)

The former is indeed the Hilbert series of
\mathbb{C}^{2}/\widehat{D}_{k}

(
Benvenuti:2006qr
, (5.31))
. This means that the Higgs branch is
\mathbb{C}^{2}/\widehat{D}_{k}
, and the Coulomb branch is trivial. The generators of the Higgs branch, corresponding to
h^{2}
,
h^{k-2}
and
h^{k-1}
, are respectively

\begin{split}w=\mathop{\rm tr}(\mu^{2})~,\quad v=X_{\left(1,\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}\right)}Q^{2k}~,\quad u=X_{\left(1,\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}\right)}Q^{2k-4}\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{3}~,\end{split}

(2.42)

satisfying the relation

\begin{split}u^{2}+v^{2}w=w^{k-1}~.\end{split}

(2.43)

Here
X_{\left(1,\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}\right)}
denotes the bare monopole operator of flux
\left(1,\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}\right)
, where it has dimension
-2
and carries the flavour charge
-4
as well as gauge charges
(-2k,2k,2k)
under the Cartan subalgebras of each
SU(2)_{i}
. The notations and contractions of the gauge indices are as described above.

2.2.2
General results for
(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})=k(\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q},-\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{r},-\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{r})
with
\mathfrak{r}=\mathfrak{p}+\mathfrak{q}

As pointed out in
(
Assel:2022row
, Footnote 7)
, the ATT condition (
2.6
) admits the general solution of the form

\begin{split}&k_{1}=\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}k~,\quad k_{2}=-\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{r}k~,\quad k_{3}=-\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{r}k~,\\
&\text{with $\mathfrak{r}=\mathfrak{p}+\mathfrak{q}$ and $\mathfrak{p},\,\mathfrak{q},\,\mathfrak{r},\,k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\neq 0}$}~.\end{split}

(2.44)

For simplicity, we will consider the cases of
\mathfrak{p}>0
,
\mathfrak{q}>0
and
k>0
. The index (
2.7
) receives non-trivial contributions from gauge fluxes
(0,0,0)
and
(\pm\mathfrak{r},\pm\mathfrak{q},\pm\mathfrak{p})n
with
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
. The contribution from flux
(0,0,0)
, up to order
x^{4}
, reads

\begin{split}1+0x+(a^{4}-1)x^{2}+a^{-2}x^{3}+(a^{8}-2)x^{4}+\ldots~.\end{split}

(2.45)

The contributions from the eight fluxes
(\pm\mathfrak{r},\pm\mathfrak{q},\pm\mathfrak{p})n
correspond to the gauge-invariant dressed monopole operators. According to the discussion around (
2.28
), it follows that the bare monopole operators associated with these fluxes have dimension
-2\mathfrak{r}n
. The charge under the flavour symmetry is
-4\mathfrak{r}n
, and the charges under the Cartan subalgebra of each
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{i}
are
2k\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{r}n(\pm 1,\mp 1,\mp 1)
. This bare monopole can be dressed with a combination of
2k\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{r}n
chiral fields of the
T_{2}
theory to form gauge invariant quantities. As a consequence, such gauge-invariant dressed monopole operators have dimension
(-2+k\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q})\mathfrak{r}n
and the charge under the flavour symmetry
(-4+2k\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q})\mathfrak{r}n
. Indeed, if
(-2+k\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q})\mathfrak{r}n
is sufficiently large, the index (
2.45
) at sufficiently low order in
x
does not get affected by these dressed monopole operators. In any case, using the same argument as above, we see from (
2.45
) that the SCFT in the IR has enhanced
{\cal N}=4
supersymmetry, regardless of the contribution of the dressed monopole operators.

In the previous example of
(-k,2k,2k)
, corresponding to
\mathfrak{p}=1
,
\mathfrak{q}=1
and
\mathfrak{r}=2
, we see that the dimension of such gauge-invariant dressed monopole operators is
(2k-4)n
and the charge under flavour symmetry is
(4k-8)n
. Indeed, for
k=3
, in addition to (
2.45
), we have the terms
a^{4}x^{2}
and
a^{8}x^{4}
coming from
n=1
and
n=2
(up to order
x^{4}
); as reported in (
2.27
). Similarly for
k=4
, we have an additional term
a^{8}x^{4}
coming from
n=1
; see (
2.27
). For
k=1
and
k=2
, the dimensions are negative (or zero) and this is why the indices diverge; as reported in (
2.27
).

One-form symmetries

Let us now discuss the one-form symmetries as well as the topological sector in the IR. From the discussion in Section
2.1
, we see that if
k
is even, then all of the CS levels
(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})
are even and it follows that the anomaly action (
2.14
) is an integral multiple of
2\pi
, which means that the corresponding anomaly theory is trivial. We thus turn to the case in which
k
is odd, in which case the anomaly theory is the same as that for
k=1
. We summarise the information in the table below.

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.77}{
\begin{tabular}[]{|c|c|c|c|c|}\hline\cr CS levels&Anomaly&Non-anomalous&Anomalous&TQFT with\\
$(\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q},-\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{r},-\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{r})$&matrix $p$ (\ref{anompmatrix})&1-form symmetry&1-form symmetry&anom. symmetry\\
\hline\cr Both $\mathfrak{p},\,\mathfrak{q}$ even&$\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}$&$\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{2}$&$\mathbf{1}$&$-$\\
\hline\cr$\mathfrak{p}$ even, $\mathfrak{q}$ odd&$\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\
1&1\end{pmatrix}$&$\mathbb{Z}_{2}$&$\mathbb{Z}_{2}$&${\cal A}^{2,1}\cong\mathrm{SU}(2)_{1}\cong\U(1)_{2}$\\
\hline\cr$\mathfrak{p}$ odd, $\mathfrak{q}$ even&$\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}$&$\mathbb{Z}_{2}$&$\mathbb{Z}_{2}$&${\cal A}^{2,1}\cong\mathrm{SU}(2)_{1}\cong\U(1)_{2}$\\
\hline\cr Both $\mathfrak{p},\,\mathfrak{q}$ odd&$\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}$&$\mathbb{Z}_{2}$&$\mathbb{Z}_{2}$&${\cal A}^{2,1}\cong\mathrm{SU}(2)_{1}\cong\U(1)_{2}$\\
\hline\cr\end{tabular}}\end{split}

(2.46)

where the third and fourth columns are given by
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{\dim(\ker\,p)}
and
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{\rank(p)}
respectively.

We thus conclude that if both
\mathfrak{p}
and
\mathfrak{q}
are even, the theory flows to an IR
{\cal N}=4
SCFT with a non-anomalous
\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{2}
one-form symmetry with no decoupled topological sector; otherwise, it flows to an IR
{\cal N}=4
SCFT with a non-anomalous
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
one-form symmetry with the decoupled
{\cal A}^{2,1}
TQFT.

2.2.3
Higgs and Coulomb branches

We can compute the Coulomb and Higgs branch limits of (
2.7
) as in (
2.37
). The result is as follows:

\begin{split}\text{Higgs limit:}&\qquad\mathop{\rm PE}\left[h^{2}+h^{K}+h^{K+1}-h^{2K+2}\right]~,\quad K=\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{r}k-2\mathfrak{r}~,\\
\text{Coulomb limit:}&\qquad 1~.\end{split}

(2.47)

These indicate that the Higgs branch is
\mathbb{C}^{2}/\widehat{D}_{K+2}
, and the Coulomb branch is a point. The generators of the Higgs branch, corresponding to
h^{2}
,
h^{K}
and
h^{K+1}
, are respectively

\begin{split}w=\mathop{\rm tr}(\mu^{2})~,\quad v=X_{(\mathfrak{r},\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p})}Q^{2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{r}k}~,\quad u=X_{(\mathfrak{r},\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p})}Q^{2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{r}k-4}\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{3}~,\end{split}

(2.48)

satisfying the relation

\begin{split}u^{2}+v^{2}w=w^{K+1}~.\end{split}

(2.49)

In the above,
X_{(\mathfrak{r},\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p})}
denotes the bare monopole operator of flux
(\mathfrak{r},\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p})
, where it has dimension
-2\mathfrak{r}
and carries the flavour charge
-4\mathfrak{r}
as well as the gauge charges
2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{r}k(1,-1,-1)
under the Cartan subalgebras of each
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{i}
. The notations and contractions of the gauge indices are as described below (
2.40
).

2.3
Cases that do not satisfy the ATT condition

In this subsection, we focus on (
2.1
) whose CS levels do not satisfy the ATT condition (
2.6
). As can be seen from the effective superpotential (
2.5
), the flavour symmetry associated with the fugacity
a
is explicitly broken, and the marginal operator
\mathop{\rm tr}(\mu^{2})
is set to zero in the chiral ring.

We will consider three interesting families of theories arising from M5-branes compactified on the quotient of the three-sphere
S^{3}/\Gamma
, where
\Gamma
is a finite subgroup of
\mathrm{SU}(2)
. The CS levels for each of these families are as follows
(
Eckhard:2019jgg
, Section 5.4.3)
:

•

Lens space
8
8

8

Recall that the Lens space
L(p,q)
can be viewed as the quotient space
S^{3}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}
with the identification
(z_{1},z_{2})\sim(e^{2\pi i/p}z_{1},e^{2\pi iq/p}z_{2})
.

L(p,q)
with
9
9

9

According to
(
Hatcher:3manifolds
, Theorem 2.5)
, the Lens spaces
L(p,q)

and
L(p^{\prime},q^{\prime})
are diffeomorphic if and only if
p^{\prime}=p
and
q^{\prime}=\pm q^{\pm 1}\,({\rm mod}\,p)
.

\frac{p}{q}=(k_{1}+1)-\frac{1}{k_{2}+1}
or
(k_{2}+1)-\frac{1}{k_{1}+1}
. Explicitly, we take

\begin{split}&\text{$p=|k_{1}k_{2}+k_{1}+k_{2}|$ and $q=\pm(k_{1}+1)$ or $\pm(k_{2}+1)$~.}\end{split}

(2.50)

This corresponds to the CS levels
(k_{1},k_{2},1)
.

•

S^{3}/D_{n}
: This corresponds to the CS levels
(-2,2,n-2)
.

•

S^{3}/E_{m}
: This corresponds to the CS levels
(-2,3,m-3)
.

We summarise the results in each case below.

1.

Let us consider the CS levels
(k_{1},k_{2},1)
. We have three cases as follows:

•

If
p=1
, i.e. the Lens space is diffeomorphic to the three-sphere, namely
L(p=1,q)\cong S^{3}
, then the index vanishes and IR theory is trivial.

•

If
p\neq 1
and both choices of
q
in (
2.50
) satisfy either of the following conditions:
10
10

10

These conditions lead to the following identifications:
(z_{1},z_{2})\sim(e^{2\pi i/p}z_{1},e^{\pm 2\pi i/p}z_{2})
or
(z_{1},z_{2})\sim(e^{2\pi i/p}z_{1},z_{2})
, with
p\neq 1
.

\begin{split}&\text{One of the $q$ is $\pm 1$ $({\rm mod}\,p)$ and the other is divisible by $p$,}\\
&\text{or both choices of $q$ are $\pm 1$ $({\rm mod}\,p)$}~,\end{split}

(2.51)

then the index
(\ref{indexsingleT2})_{a=1}
is equal to unity, and so theory (
2.1
) flows to a TQFT.

•

Otherwise, the index
(\ref{indexsingleT2})_{a=1}
takes the form

\begin{split}1+0x-x^{2}+2x^{3}+\ldots~,\end{split}

(2.52)

where
0x
indicates that there is no
{\cal N}=3
flavour current and
-x^{2}
indicates that there is one
{\cal N}=3
extra SUSY-current; therefore, each theory in this subclass flows to a
3d
{\cal N}=4
interacting SCFT
, where supersymmetry gets
enhanced
in the IR, with a decoupled TQFT.

Using the information in Section
2.1
, we summarise the information about the anomalies and TQFTs below.
11
11

11

As before, the third and fourth columns are given by
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{\dim(\ker\,p)}
and
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{\rank(p)}
respectively. These deserve some explanations. Let us consider, for example, the second row, i.e. that with
({\cal Z}_{2})_{2}
TQFT. The matrix element
1
in the first row and first column means the first
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
one-form symmetry is individually anomalous, in the sense that
\pi(k_{1}+1)\int_{{\cal M}_{4}}{\cal P}(w_{1}^{(2)})/2\equiv\pi\int_{{\cal M}_{4}}{\cal P}(w_{1}^{(2)})/2
in (
2.14
) is non-trivial. The matrix element
0
means that the second
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
one-form symmetry is individually non-anomalous. The off-diagonal element 1 means that there is a mixed anomaly between the first and second
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetries. If one takes the diagonal subgroup of these two
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
, namely setting
w_{1}^{(2)}=w_{2}^{(2)}\equiv B^{(2)}
, it is anomalous due to the non-trivial term
\pi(k_{1}+1)\int_{{\cal M}_{4}}{\cal P}(B^{(2)})/2-2\pi\int_{{\cal M}_{4}}{\cal P}(B^{(2)})/2=\pi(k_{1}-1)\int_{{\cal M}_{4}}{\cal P}(B^{(2)})/2
in (
2.14
). We will also discuss this below (
2.59
). Here,
\ker(p)=\emptyset
means that any two
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetries associated with linearly independent combinations of
w_{1}^{(2)}
and
w_{2}^{(2)}
(with the coefficients in
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
) will be involved in an individual and/or a mixed ’t Hooft anomaly. This should be contrasted with the last row: the vector
(-1,1)^{T}\equiv(1,1)^{T}\in\ker(p)
means that the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
one-form symmetry associated with
w_{1}^{(2)}+w_{2}^{(2)}
is free from both individual and mixed ’t Hooft anomalies.

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.76}{
\begin{tabular}[]{|c|c|c|c|c|}\hline\cr CS levels&Anomaly&Non-anomalous&Anomalous&TQFT with\\
$(k_{1},k_{2},1)$&matrix $p$&1-form symmetry&1-form symmetry&anom. symmetry\\
\hline\cr Both $k_{1}$ and&$\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{pmatrix}$&$\mathbf{1}$&$\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{2}$&${\cal A}^{\{2,2\},p}\equiv({\cal Z}_{2})_{0}$\\
$k_{2}$ are odd&&&&in the notation of \cite[cite]{\@@bibref{Authors Phrase1YearPhrase2}{Hsin:2018vcg}{\@@citephrase{(}}{\@@citephrase{)}}}\\
\hline\cr$k_{1}$ is even&$\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\
1&0\end{pmatrix}$&$\mathbf{1}$&$\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{2}$&${\cal A}^{\{2,2\},p}\equiv({\cal Z}_{2})_{2}$\\
and $k_{2}$ is odd&&&&\\
\hline\cr Both $k_{1}$ and&$\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\
1&1\end{pmatrix}$&$\mathbb{Z}_{2}$&$\mathbb{Z}_{2}$&${\cal A}^{2,1}\cong\mathrm{SU}(2)_{1}$\\
$k_{2}$ are even&&&&$\cong\U(1)_{2}$\\
\hline\cr\end{tabular}}\end{split}

(2.53)

Note that, in the case of
k_{1}
and
k_{2}
even, theory (
2.1
) flows to either

•

an
{\cal N}=4
SCFT with a non-anomalous
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
one-form symmetry
\otimes

{\cal A}^{2,1}
, or

•

a TQFT with non-anomalous
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
one-form symmetry
\otimes

{\cal A}^{2,1}

depending on the value of
p/q
as discussed above.

2.

Let us consider the CS levels
(-2,2,n-2)
with
n\geq 4
.
12
12

12

The case of
n=3
was discussed in Case

.
The index
(\ref{indexsingleT2})_{a=1}
is unity. This indicates that the theory flows to a TQFT in the IR.

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.8}{
\begin{tabular}[]{|c|c|c|c|c|}\hline\cr CS levels&Anomaly&Non-anomalous&Anomalous&TQFT with\\

(2.54)

For
n
even, theory (
2.1
) flows to a TQFT that has a non-anomalous
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{2}
one-form symmetry, whereas for
n

\mathbb{Z}_{2}
one-form symmetry
\otimes

{\cal A}^{2,1}
.

3.

Let us consider the CS levels
(-2,3,m-3)
with

. The index
(\ref{indexsingleT2})_{a=1}
is unity, and so theory (
2.1
) flows to a TQFT in the IR.

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.8}{
\begin{tabular}[]{|c|c|c|c|c|}\hline\cr CS levels&Anomaly&Non-anomalous&Anomalous&TQFT with\\
&matrix $p$&1-form symmetry&1-form symmetry&anom. symmetry\\
\hline\cr$m$ even&$\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\

\hline\cr\end{tabular}}\end{split}

(2.55)

For
m
even, theory (
2.1
) flows to the TQFT
{\cal A}^{\{2,2\},p}\equiv({\cal Z}_{2})_{2}
, whereas for
m
odd, it flows to a TQFT that has a non-anomalous
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
one-form symmetry
\otimes

{\cal A}^{2,1}
.

2.3.1
Special case of
(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})=(k,1,1)

For any integer
k
, these CS levels do
not
satisfy the ATT condition.
13
13

13

Other CS levels that leads to the same IR SCFTs in accordance with Footnote
9
are, for example,
(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})=(-k-1,1,1)
and
(k-1,-3,1)
. We have checked that the indices of the corresponding theories are equal. However, if
k
is even, the topological sector for
(k,1,1)
is
({\cal Z}_{2})_{2}
, whereas that for
(-k-1,1,1)
and
(k-1,-3,1)
is
({\cal Z}_{2})_{0}
. On the other hand, if
k
is odd, the situation is reverse: the topological sector for
(k,1,1)
is
({\cal Z}_{2})_{0}
and that for
(-k-1,1,1)
and
(k-1,-3,1)
is
({\cal Z}_{2})_{2}
. We expect that different topological sectors (despite the diffeomorphism of the three-manifolds) arise from different choices of polarisation of the 6d
{\cal N}=(2,0)
theory; see also
Eckhard:2019jgg
.

Let us report the indices, up to order
x^{10}
, for the cases of
k\geq 1
below:

\begin{split}\begin{tabular}[]{|c|c|}\hline\cr$k$&Index\\
\hline\cr$1$&$1$\\
$2$&$1-x^{2}+2x^{3}-2x^{4}+2x^{5}-2x^{6}+2x^{7}-2x^{8}+2x^{10}+\ldots$\\
$3$&$1-x^{2}+2x^{3}-2x^{4}+x^{5}+x^{8}-4x^{9}+7x^{10}+\ldots$\\
$\geq 4$&$1-x^{2}+2x^{3}-2x^{4}+x^{5}-2x^{9}+5x^{10}+\ldots$\\
\hline\cr\end{tabular}\end{split}

(2.56)

where for
k\geq 4
the indices differ from each other at higher order than
x^{10}
.

For
k=1
, the theory flows to the TQFT given by the first row of (
2.53
). For
k\geq 2
, each of these theories flows to an
interacting SCFT with enhanced
{\cal N}=4
supersymmetry
, along with a decoupled TQFT, in the IR.
14
14

14

After this paper had been published in the JHEP, Ref.
Gang:2023rei
appeared on the arXiv. A class of 3d
{\cal N}=4
rank-zero SCFTs was proposed. Each of them (labelled by
r\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
) admits a Lagrangian description in terms of a 3d
{\cal N}=2
CS-matter theory with
r

\U(1)
gauge groups, CS couplings between them, chiral multiplets carrying charge
+1
under each gauge group, and a monopole superpotential. Supersymmetry of such a theory gets enhanced to
{\cal N}=4
in the IR. We find that the index of the theory labelled by
r=k-1
is equal to that of our CS-
T_{2}
theory with CS levels
(k,1,1)
listed in (
2.56
), up to a very high order in
x
. As an example, for
r=2
, the index of the theory in
Gang:2023rei
is given by

\begin{split}&\sum_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\oint\frac{dz_{1}}{2\pi iz_{1}}\oint\frac{dz_{2}}{2\pi iz_{2}}z_{1}^{(2-\frac{1}{2})m_{1}}z_{2}^{(4-\frac{1}{2})m_{2}}z_{1}^{2m_{2}}z_{2}^{2m_{1}}v^{m_{1}}v^{2m_{2}}\times\\
&\qquad\qquad{\cal Z}^{R=1}_{\chi}(x^{\mathfrak{r}_{1}-1}z_{1},m_{1};x){\cal Z}^{R=1}_{\chi}(x^{\mathfrak{r}_{2}-1}z_{2},m_{2};x)~,\qquad\mathfrak{r}_{1}=-1,\,\mathfrak{r}_{2}=1\\
&=1-x^{2}+(v+v^{-1})x^{3}-2x^{4}+vx^{5}+(v^{-2}-1)x^{6}+(v-v^{-1})x^{7}+\\
&\qquad\quad v^{-2}x^{8}-(v+3v^{-1})x^{9}+(4+v^{2}+2v^{-2})x^{10}+\ldots~.\end{split}

Upon setting
v=1
, we obtain the index (
2.56
) with
k=3
. An advantage of such a Lagrangian description is that one can turn on the fugacity
v
associated with the manifest
\U(1)
global symmetry in the
{\cal N}=2
theory. The conserved current of this symmetry plays a role as the
{\cal N}=3
extra SUSY-current for our CS-
T_{2}
theory. We thank Dongmin Gang for a useful discussion and for comparing the result in
Gang:2023rei
with ours during the workshop “Recent Trends in Supersymmetric Field Theories 2023”.
The enhancement of supersymmetry from
{\cal N}=3
to
{\cal N}=4
can be deduced using the same argument as in the precedent subsection: due to the absence of the
{\cal N}=2
preserving marginal operator, the term
-x^{2}
indicates that there is one
{\cal N}=3
extra SUSY-current, rendering the enhanced supersymmetry. From (
2.53
), we see that the interacting SCFT does not have a non-anomalous one-form symmetry, and the decoupled TQFT has an anomalous
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{2}
one-form symmetry whose anomaly is given by the first or second row of (
2.53
) depending whether
k
is odd or even.

The case of
k=2

The case of
k=2
is of particular importance: the SCFT in question turns out to be the (rank-zero) minimal 3d
{\cal N}=4
SCFT, discussed in
Gang:2018huc
;
Gang:2021hrd
.
From (
2.7
), the index of this theory, up to order
x^{12}
, is

\begin{split}1-x^{2}+2x^{3}-2x^{4}+2x^{5}-2x^{6}+2x^{7}-2x^{8}+2x^{10}-2x^{11}+3x^{12}+\ldots~.\end{split}

(2.57)

This turns out to be equal to the index of the (rank-zero) minimal 3d
{\cal N}=4
SCFT, described by 3d
{\cal N}=2

\U(1)_{-3/2}
gauge theory with one chiral multiplet of charge 1
Gang:2018huc
;
Gang:2021hrd
, namely

\begin{split}&\oint\frac{dz}{2\pi iz}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}w^{m}z^{-\frac{3}{2}m}{\cal Z}^{1/3}_{\chi}(z,m;x)\\
&=1-x^{2}+(w+w^{-1})x^{3}-2x^{4}+(w+w^{-1})x^{5}-2x^{6}+\\
&\qquad(w+w^{-1})x^{7}-2x^{8}+(w^{2}+w^{-2})x^{10}-(w+w^{-1})x^{11}+\\
&\qquad(w^{2}+1+w^{-2})x^{12}+\ldots~,\end{split}

(2.58)

upon setting the fugacity
w
for the topological symmetry to
1
. It was pointed out in
Gang:2018huc
that, in the
\U(1)_{-3/2}
CS theory, supersymmetry gets enhanced from
{\cal N}=2
to
{\cal N}=4
in the IR. Viewing these as
{\cal N}=2
indices, the absence of the term at order
x
and the positive term at order order
x^{2}
implies that there is no
{\cal N}=2
preserving relevant and marginal deformation. As pointed out by
Razamat:2016gzx
;
Evtikhiev:2017heo
(see also
Cordova:2016emh
), at order
x^{3}
, there are two
{\cal N}=2
extra SUSY-current multiplets
A_{1}\overline{A}_{1}[1]_{3/2}^{(0)}
that render supersymmetry enhancement. Here we find a
new description
, in terms of the
{\cal N}=3
gauge theory (
2.1
) with the CS levels
(2,1,1)
, of the minimal 3d
{\cal N}=4
SCFT, along with the decoupled TQFT described in the second row of (
2.53
). The
{\cal N}=3
extra SUSY-current of this theory should actually be identified with the current of the topological symmetry that is manifest in the 3d
{\cal N}=2

\U(1)_{-3/2}
CS theory.

In fact, there is another theory that has a similar behaviour: the 3d
{\cal N}=2

\U(1)_{0}
gauge theory with one chiral multiplet of charge
2
. This was, in fact, mentioned in
(
Gang:2018huc
, (36))
. The three-sphere partition function of this theory is
Z=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}s\,\Gamma_{h}(ir+2s)
,
15
15

15

Here we use the same convention as
(
Aharony:2013dha
, Section 5.1)
and turn off the FI and mass parameters.
where
r
is the R-charge of the chiral multiplet. It turns out that the value of
|Z|
is independent of
r
such that
0\leq r<1
, and we find that the free energy is
F\equiv-\log|Z|=-\log\sqrt{\frac{5-\sqrt{5}}{10}}+\log\sqrt{2}
. According to
(
Gang:2021hrd
, (3.6))
, the free energy of the minimal 3d
{\cal N}=4
SCFT is
-\log\sqrt{\frac{5-\sqrt{5}}{10}}
. The other contribution comes from the TQFT
{\cal A}^{2,1}\cong\U(1)_{2}
, whose free energy is given by
-\log\left|\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}s\,e^{2\pi is^{2}}\right|=\log\sqrt{2}
. Hence, we conclude that the 3d
{\cal N}=2

\U(1)_{0}
gauge theory with one chiral multiplet of charge
2
flows to the minimal 3d
{\cal N}=4
SCFT along with the
{\cal A}^{2,1}
TQFT. Indeed, the index of the
\U(1)_{0}
gauge theory is given by the integral
\oint\frac{dz}{2\pi iz}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}w^{m}{\cal Z}^{r}_{\chi}(z^{2},2m;x)
and the result turns out to be equal to (
2.58
).

Description in terms of the
\USp(2)_{k}\times\mathrm{Spin}(4)_{1}
gauge theory

Theory (
2.1
) with the CS levels
(k,1,1)
can also be described by the 3d
{\cal N}=3

\USp(2)_{k}\times\mathrm{Spin}(4)_{1}
gauge theory with a half-hypermultiplet in the representation
[\mathbf{4};\mathbf{2}]
. In fact, it can be checked that the index of this theory is equal to that of the
\USp(2)_{k}\times\mathrm{SO}(4)_{1}
gauge theory with the same matter content. This is because in the latter the bare monopole operators cannot be dressed by the half-hypermultiplet to form a gauge invariant operator. Since the fugacity
\zeta
of the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
zero-form magnetic symmetry does not appear in the index of the
\USp(2)_{k}\times\mathrm{SO}(4)_{1}
gauge theory, this means it acts trivially on the local operators. This indeed signalises that the corresponding dual one-form symmetry in the
\USp(2)_{k}\times\mathrm{Spin}(4)_{1}
gauge theory acts on the line operators of the decoupled topological sector, in accordance with the above statement that the interacting SCFT does not have a non-anomalous
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
one-form symmetry. Note that the topological sector is invisible to the index computation (
2.7
).

The
\USp(2)_{k}\times\mathrm{SO}(4)_{1}
gauge theory may have a non-anomalous
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
one-form symmetry, depending on
k
. As before, the condition for the existence of a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
one-form symmetry of this theory can be determined by a simple generalisation of
(
Tachikawa:2019dvq
, (3.27))
(see Footnote
4
with
k_{1}=1
,
M=2
,
k_{2}=k
and
N=1
):

\begin{split}\frac{1}{4}\times 1\times 2+\frac{1}{2}k=\frac{1}{2}(k+1)\in\mathbb{Z}\qquad\Leftrightarrow\qquad\text{$k$ is odd}~.\end{split}

(2.59)

We interpret this results as follows. Although theory (
2.1
) with the CS levels
(k,1,1)
, or equivalently the
\USp(2)_{k}\times\mathrm{Spin}(4)_{1}
gauge theory, has an anomalous
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{2}
one-form symmetry, its
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
diagonal subgroup is non-anomalous for
k
odd. This can be seen directly from the action (
2.14
) of the anomaly theory:
S_{\text{anom}}=-\frac{2\pi}{2}\int_{{\cal M}_{4}}(w_{1}^{(2)}\cup w_{2}^{(2)})
, where the first two terms of (
2.14
) can be dropped. Upon taking
w_{1}^{(2)}=w_{2}^{(2)}\equiv B^{(2)}
, we have
S_{\text{anom}}=-2\pi\int_{{\cal M}_{4}}{\cal P}(B^{(2)})/2
. Since
\int_{{\cal M}_{4}}{\cal P}(B^{(2)})
is even on a spin manifold
{{\cal M}_{4}}
, this action is an integer multiple of
2\pi
; this indicates that the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
diagonal subgroup of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{2}
one-form symmetry is non-anomalous for
k
odd. On the other hand, for
k
even, we also have a non-trivial contribution from the first term of (
2.14
), namely
\pi(k-1)\int_{{\cal M}_{4}}{\cal P}(w_{1}^{(2)})/2
; this renders the anomaly of the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
diagonal subgroup non-trivial.

For
k
odd, the above interpretation can be supported by an explicit realisation of the topological sector
({\cal Z}_{2})_{0}
; see the first row of (
2.53
). As discussed around
(
Hsin:2018vcg
, (2.7))
, the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{2}
one-form symmetry of the
({\cal Z}_{2})_{0}
TQFT is generated by the basic electric and magnetic lines
V_{E}
,
V_{M}
of integer spins, where each of such lines generates a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
non-anomalous one-form symmetry labelled by
p=0
. Due to a non-trivial mutual braiding phase
e^{-i\pi}
of
V_{E}
and
V_{M}
, we can always find a line
b
that generates a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
subgroup of the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{2}
one-form symmetry with anomaly characterised by
p
(with
p=0,\,1\,\,{\rm mod}\,2
), namely
b=V_{E}^{p/2}V_{M}
. We see that the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
diagonal subgroup, generated by the line
V_{E}V_{M}
, of the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{2}
one-form symmetry is indeed anomaly free.

2.4
Gluing with
T(\mathrm{SU}(2))
theories

Let us now include the
T(\mathrm{SU}(2))
SCFT
Gaiotto:2008ak
into the discussion. This theory can be realised as an IR SCFT of the 3d
{\cal N}=4

\U(1)
gauge theory with two hypermultiplets of charge
1
and it has an
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{H}\times\mathrm{SU}(2)_{C}
flavour symmetry with the mixed anomaly given by the following anomaly theory (see
Gang:2018wek
;
Genolini:2022mpi
;
Bhardwaj:2022dyt
and also
Komargodski:2017dmc
)

\begin{split}\pi\int_{{\cal M}_{4}}w_{2}^{H}\cup w_{2}^{C}~,\end{split}

(2.60)

where
w_{2}^{H}
and
w_{2}^{C}
are, respectively, the second Stiefel-Whitney classes associated with the
\mathrm{SO}(3)_{H}
and
\mathrm{SO}(3)_{C}
bundles that obstruct the lift to the
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{H}
and
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{C}
bundles.

As discussed in
Assel:2022row
, an interesting generalisation of (
2.1
) is to gauge the diagonal subgroup of the
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{i}
symmetries (with
i=1,2,3
) of the
T_{2}
theory and the
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{C}
global symmetry of the
i
-th copy of the
T(SU(2))
theory with CS level
k^{(1)}_{i}
, and then gauge the
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{H}
global symmetry of the
i
-th copy of the
T(\mathrm{SU}(2))
theory with CS level
k^{(2)}_{i}
. The resulting theory can be represented as

where

(2.62)

For simplicity, we focus on the configuration (
2.61
). As pointed out in
(
Assel:2022row
, Section 3.3)
, this model can be realised by compactifying M5-branes on a three-manifold given by a Seifert bundle over
S^{2}
with three singular fibers, with Seifert parameters
q_{1}/p_{1}
,
q_{2}/p_{2}
and
q_{3}/p_{3}
, where

\begin{split}\frac{p_{i}}{q_{i}}=k^{(1)}_{i}-\frac{1}{k^{(2)}_{i}}~,\qquad\text{$i=1,2,3$}~.\end{split}

(2.63)

The effective superpotential after integrating out the adjoint scalars is

\begin{split}W=\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{q_{1}}{p_{1}}+\frac{q_{2}}{p_{2}}+\frac{q_{3}}{p_{3}}\right)\mathop{\rm tr}(\mu^{2})+\sum_{i=1}^{3}\frac{q_{i}}{p_{i}}\mathop{\rm tr}(\mu_{i}\mu_{i,C})~,\end{split}

(2.64)

where
\mu_{i}^{C}
denotes the moment map of the
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{C}
global symmetry of the
i
-th copy of the
T(\mathrm{SU}(2))
theory, and
\mu_{i}
is the moment map of the
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{i}
global symmetry of the
T_{2}
theory satisfying (
2.4
). The ATT condition (
2.6
) is then generalised to
Assel:2022row

\begin{split}\frac{q_{1}}{p_{1}}+\frac{q_{2}}{p_{2}}+\frac{q_{3}}{p_{3}}=0~.\end{split}

(2.65)

Similarly to (
2.1
), we will again show that this is a
sufficient
condition for supersymmetry enhancement in the IR. However, even if (
2.65
) is not satisfied, there are cases in which the IR SCFT has accidental
{\cal N}=4
supersymmetry.

The index of the
T(\mathrm{SU}(2))
SCFT can be written as

\begin{split}&{\cal I}_{T(\mathrm{SU}(2))}(w,n|f,m|a,n_{a};x)\\
&=\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}(w^{2})^{l}\oint\frac{dz}{2\pi iz}z^{n}{\cal Z}_{\chi}^{1}(a^{-2};-2n_{a};x)\times\\
&\qquad\quad\prod_{s=\pm 1}{\cal Z}_{\chi}^{1/2}((zf)^{s}a;s(l+m)+n_{a};x){\cal Z}_{\chi}^{1/2}((z^{-1}f)^{s}a;s(-l+m)+n_{a};x)~,\end{split}

(2.66)

where
(w,n)
are the (fugacity, background magnetic flux) for the topological symmetry,
(f,m)
are those for the flavour symmetry, and
(a,n_{a})
are those for the axial symmetry. Here we normalise the power of the fugacity
w
in such a way that the elementary monopole operators
V_{\pm}
carry the fugacity
a^{-2}w^{\pm 2}
. In this way, the Coulomb branch moment maps correspond to the term
a^{-2}\chi^{\mathfrak{su}(2)_{C}}_{[2]}(w)x
in the index, and the Higgs branch moment maps correspond to the term
a^{2}\chi^{\mathfrak{su}(2)_{H}}_{[2]}(f)x
. The index for theory (
2.61
) is therefore given by

\begin{split}&{\cal I}_{(\ref{singleT2withTSU2})}(a,n_{a};x)\\
&=\left(\frac{1}{8}\prod_{i=1}^{3}\oint\frac{dz_{i}}{2\pi iz_{i}}\right)\sum_{(m_{1},\cdots,m_{3})\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\left(\frac{1}{8}\prod_{i=1}^{3}\oint\frac{df_{i}}{2\pi if_{i}}\right)\sum_{(\widehat{m}_{1},\cdots,\widehat{m}_{3})\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\times\\
&\quad\ \left(\prod_{i=1}^{3}z_{i}^{2k^{(1)}_{i}m_{i}}{\cal Z}^{\mathrm{SU}(2)}_{\text{vec}}(z_{i};m_{i};x)\right)\left(\prod_{i=1}^{3}f_{i}^{2k^{(2)}_{i}\widehat{m}_{i}}{\cal Z}^{\mathrm{SU}(2)}_{\text{vec}}(f_{i};\widehat{m}_{i};x)\right)\times\\
&\quad\ \prod_{s_{1},s_{2},s_{3}=\pm 1}{\cal Z}^{1/2}_{\chi}(z_{1}^{s_{1}}z_{2}^{s_{2}}z_{3}^{s_{3}}a;s_{1}m_{1}+s_{2}m_{2}+s_{3}m_{3}+n_{a};x)\times\\
&\quad\ \prod_{i=1}^{3}{\cal I}_{T(\mathrm{SU}(2))}(z_{i},m_{i}|f_{i},\widehat{m}_{i}|a,n_{a};x)~.\end{split}

(2.67)

When the ATT condition (
2.65
) is satisfied, the first term in (
2.64
) vanishes and the
\U(1)_{a}
symmetry associated with the fugacity
a
assigned as above is a symmetry of the theory, since
\mu_{i}
carries charge
+2
and
\mu_{i,C}
carries charge
-2
. However, if (
2.65
) is not satisfied, we set
a=1
and
n_{a}=0
in the above expression of the index.

2.4.1
’t Hooft anomalies of the one-form symmetries

Gauging the
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{H}
and
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{C}
global symmetries of
T(\mathrm{SU}(2))
respectively with CS levels
k_{H}
and
k_{C}
leads to the
\mathbb{Z}_{2,H}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2,C}
one-form symmetry arising from the centres of
SU(2)_{H}
and
SU(2)_{C}
. The ’t Hooft anomaly of such a one-form symmetry is characterised by the following anomaly theory (see
(
Gang:2021hrd
, (3.62))
)

\begin{split}\pi\int_{{\cal M}_{4}}\left[k_{H}\frac{{\cal P}(w_{H}^{(2)})}{2}+k_{C}\frac{{\cal P}(w_{C}^{(2)})}{2}+w_{H}^{(2)}\cup w_{C}^{(2)}\right]~,\end{split}

(2.68)

where
w_{H/C}^{(2)}
are the two-form background fields for the
\mathbb{Z}_{2,H/C}
one-form symmetries. The first two terms arise as in (
2.11
) and the last term comes from (
2.60
). Upon gauging with the
T_{2}
theory as in (
2.61
), we have six
\mathrm{SU}(2)
gauge groups but
\mathbb{Z}^{5}_{2}
one-form symmetry due to the screening effect of the matter of the
T_{2}
theory. The ’t Hooft anomalies of the latter are given by

\begin{split}\pi\int_{{\cal M}_{4}}\left[\sum_{r=1}^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{3}k^{(r)}_{i}\frac{{\cal P}(B_{i}^{(r)})}{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{3}B_{i}^{(1)}\cup B_{i}^{(2)}\right]\quad\text{with}~\sum_{i=1}^{3}B_{i}^{(1)}=0~,\end{split}

(2.69)

where
B_{i}^{(r)}
is the two-form background field associated with the
\mathrm{SU}(2)
gauge group with CS level
k_{i}^{(r)}
, with
i=1,2,3
and
r=1,2
.
16
16

16

We denote the two-form background fields for the one-form symmetries differently from the other sections in order to avoid cluttered notation.
The last constraint comes from (
2.12
).

2.4.2
Summary of the results

We observe the following result:

\begin{split}&\text{For given ratios $p_{i}/q_{i}$ (with $i=1,2,3$), the index (\ref{indsingleT2withTSU2}) of theory}\\
&\text{(\ref{singleT2withTSU2}) is independent of specific values of $k^{(a_{i})}_{i}$ in (\ref{defratios}).}\end{split}

(2.70)

As an immediate consequence, the following statement holds:

\begin{split}&\text{If $p_{i}/q_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}$ for all $i=1,2,3$, the index (\ref{indsingleT2withTSU2}) of theory (\ref{singleT2withTSU2})}\\
&\text{is equal to the index (\ref{indexsingleT2}) of theory (\ref{singleT2}) with $k_{i}=p_{i}/q_{i}$.}\end{split}

(2.71)

We remark that these statements are true, independently of whether the ATT condition (
2.65
) is satisfied. This means that the aforementioned theories flow to the same interacting SCFT in the IR. This observation may not be a surprise from the geometrical perspective, since both theories are associated with Seifert manifolds that are diffeomorphic to each other. Note that the decoupled topological sectors in the IR may be different, since the anomalous one-form symmetries determined by (
2.14
) and (
2.69
) may be different. We have actually seen this phenomenon in Footnote
13
.

(2.72)

with various CS levels as follows.

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.94}{
\begin{tabular}[]{|c|c|c|c|c|c|c|}\hline\cr$k^{(1)}_{1}$&$k^{(2)}_{1}$&$\frac{p_{1}}{q_{1}}$&$k_{2}$&$k_{3}$&ATT (\ref{ATT2})&Index\\
\hline\cr$-4$&$-1$&$-3$&$6$&$6$&✓&(\ref{indmk2k2k}) with $k=3$\\
\hline\cr$3$&$2$&$\frac{5}{2}$&$-5$&$-5$&✓&$1+(2a^{4}-1)x^{2}+(a^{6}-a^{2}+a^{-2})x^{3}+$\\
$2$&$-2$&&&&&$(3a^{8}-a^{4}-2)x^{4}+\ldots$\\
\hline\cr$3$&$3$&$\frac{8}{3}$&$-4$&$-8$&✓&$1+(a^{4}-1)x^{2}+a^{-2}x^{3}$ +\\
&&&&&&$2(a^{8}-1)x^{4}+\ldots$\\
\hline\cr$2$&$2$&$\frac{3}{2}$&$-3$&$-3$&✓&diverges\\
\hline\cr\hline\cr$1$&$3$&$\frac{2}{3}$&$1$&$1$&✗&$1+0x-x^{2}+2x^{3}-2x^{4}+\ldots$\\
\hline\cr$2$&$2$&$\frac{3}{2}$&$1$&$1$&✗&$1+0x-x^{2}+2x^{3}-2x^{4}+\ldots$\\
$1$&$-2$&&&&&\\
\hline\cr$-2$&$-2$&$-\frac{3}{2}$&$1$&$1$&✗&$1$\\
\hline\cr$1$&$7$&$\frac{6}{7}$&$-2$&$3$&✗&$1+0x+0x^{2}+x^{3}-x^{4}+\ldots$\\
\hline\cr\end{tabular}}\end{split}

(2.73)

For reference, we also provide the contribution of zero gauge magnetic fluxes:

\begin{split}1+0x+(a^{4}-1)x^{2}+a^{-2}x^{3}+(a^{8}-2)x^{4}+\ldots~.\end{split}

(2.74)

The term
a^{4}x^{2}
indicates that there is one marginal operator coming from the zero gauge flux sector, the term
0x
indicates the absence of the
{\cal N}=3
flavour symmetry current, and so the term
-x^{2}
indicates that there is one
{\cal N}=3
extra SUSY-current. Therefore, when the ATT condition is satisfied, the IR SCFT has enhanced
{\cal N}=4
supersymmetry. However, when the index diverges, we cannot conclude the IR behaviour from it.

When the ATT condition is not satisfied, there are cases in which the IR SCFTs have
enhanced
{\cal N}=4
supersymmetry
; these are explicitly shown in the fifth and sixth rows of Table (
2.73
). This can be deduced by the same reasoning as above. Note that the marginal operator discussed below (
2.74
) is set to zero in the chiral ring by an
F
-term equation (
cf.
Section
2.3
) and so we do not have a positive term at order
x^{2}
. However, in the final row of Table (
2.73
), we find no indication of supersymmetry enhancement from the index, assuming that there is no additional marginal operator. If the index is unity, then the IR theory is a TQFT.

Next, let us report some indices for theory (
2.61
) with various CS levels such that the ATT condition (
2.65
) is satisfied:

\begin{split}\begin{tabular}[]{|c|c|c|c|c|c|c|c|c|c|}\hline\cr$k^{(1)}_{1}$&$k^{(2)}_{1}$&$\frac{p_{1}}{q_{1}}$&$k^{(1)}_{2}$&$k^{(2)}_{2}$&$\frac{p_{2}}{q_{2}}$&$k^{(1)}_{3}$&$k^{(2)}_{3}$&$\frac{p_{3}}{q_{3}}$&Index\\
\hline\cr$-2$&$1$&$-3$&$7$&$1$&$6$&$7$&$1$&$6$&(\ref{indmk2k2k}) with $k=3$\\
\hline\cr$-3$&$1$&$-4$&$9$&$1$&$8$&$9$&$1$&$8$&(\ref{indmk2k2k}) with $k=4$\\
\hline\cr$-4$&$1$&$-5$&$11$&$1$&$10$&$11$&$1$&$10$&(\ref{indmk2k2k}) with $k=5$\\
\hline\cr\end{tabular}\end{split}

(2.75)

These results support the statement (
2.71
). In all of these cases, the IR interacting SCFT has enhanced
{\cal N}=4
supersymmetry.

Finally, we observe that if a pair of Seifert fibres with parameters
q_{i}/p_{i}
and
\widetilde{q}_{i}/\widetilde{p}_{i}
are isomorphic by an orientation-preserving diffeomorphism, namely
(
Hatcher:3manifolds
, Prop. 2.1)

1.

after possibly permuting indices,
q_{i}/p_{i}=\widetilde{q}_{i}/\widetilde{p}_{i}

({\rm mod}\,1)
for each
i
, and

2.

\sum_{i}q_{i}/p_{i}=\sum_{i}\widetilde{q}_{i}/\widetilde{p}_{i}
,

then the indices of the theories associated with these Seifert fibres are equal. In other words, the corresponding IR SCFTs are the same.

T_{2}
building blocks

}{{{{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}{{}}}{{{{}}{

{}{}{}{{{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}}
{{{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setdash{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 12.11 -6.99 C 43.82 -25.29 74.29 -25.29 106 -6.99}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
