{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{38.99405pt}{-10.43999pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{65}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\end{split}

(3.1)

We denote by
\mu_{i}^{(I)}
, with
i=1,2,3
and
I=1,2
, the moment maps of the
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{i}
flavour symmetry of the
I
-th
T_{2}
theory. Their explicit expression for each
I
is given by (
2.3
). We also have the analogue of (
2.4
), namely

\begin{split}\mathop{\rm tr}(\mu^{(I)\,\,2}_{1})=\mathop{\rm tr}(\mu^{(I)\,\,2}_{2})=\mathop{\rm tr}(\mu^{(I)\,\,2}_{3})\equiv\mathop{\rm tr}(\mu^{(I)\,\,2})~\end{split}

(3.2)

for each
I=1,2
. The effective superpotential after integrating out the adjoint scalar fields is (see
(
Assel:2022row
, (2.16))
)

\begin{split}W&=\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{1}{k_{1}}+\frac{1}{k_{2}}+\frac{1}{k_{3}}\right)\left[\mathop{\rm tr}\left(\mu^{(1)\,\,2}\right)+\mathop{\rm tr}\left(\mu^{(2)\,\,2}\right)\right]+\sum_{i=1}^{3}\frac{1}{k_{i}}\mathop{\rm tr}\left(\mu_{i}^{(1)}\mu_{i}^{(2)}\right)~.\end{split}

(3.3)

When the ATT condition (
2.6
) is satisfied, the first term vanishes and there is a flavour symmetry that assigns charge
+1
to every chiral field of the first
T_{2}
theory and charge
-1
to every chiral field of the second
T_{2}
theory. As a consequence,
\mu_{i}^{(1)}
and
\mu_{i}^{(2)}
carry charges
+2
and
-2
, respectively. We denote by
a
the fugacity associated with this flavour symmetry. We will shortly see that, if the CS levels obey the ATT condition, the flavour symmetry algebra is actually
\mathfrak{su}(2)_{a}
. On the other hand, if the ATT condition (
2.6
) is not satisfied, this flavour symmetry is explicitly broken by the first term of the superpotential.

The index for theory (
3.1
) is given by

\begin{split}{\cal I}_{(\ref{twoT2})}(a,n_{a};x)&=\left(\frac{1}{8}\prod_{i=1}^{3}\oint\frac{dz_{i}}{2\pi iz_{i}}\right)\sum_{(m_{1},m_{2},m_{3})\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{3}z_{i}^{2k_{i}m_{i}}{\cal Z}^{\mathrm{SU}(2)}_{\text{vec}}(z_{i};m_{i};x)\right)\times\\
&\qquad\prod_{s_{1},s_{2},s_{3}=\pm 1}{\cal Z}^{1/2}_{\chi}(z_{1}^{s_{1}}z_{2}^{s_{2}}z_{3}^{s_{3}}a;s_{1}m_{1}+s_{2}m_{2}+s_{3}m_{3}+n_{a};x)\times\\
&\qquad\prod_{s^{\prime}_{1},s^{\prime}_{2},s^{\prime}_{3}=\pm 1}{\cal Z}^{1/2}_{\chi}(z_{1}^{s^{\prime}_{1}}z_{2}^{s^{\prime}_{2}}z_{3}^{s^{\prime}_{3}}a^{-1};s^{\prime}_{1}m_{1}+s^{\prime}_{2}m_{2}+s^{\prime}_{3}m_{3}-n_{a};x)~.\end{split}

(3.4)

As before, we will set
n_{a}=0
and drop
n_{a}
from the argument in
{\cal I}_{(\ref{twoT2})}(a,n_{a};x)
when we study the series expansion of the index. For the cases that do not satisfy the ATT condition,
a
should be set to
1
and
n_{a}
should be set to zero.

Without the CS levels, theory (
3.1
) can be viewed as 3d reduction of the 4d
{\cal N}=2

A_{1}
class
{\cal S}
theory associated with a Riemann surface of genus 2 with no puncture. The one-form symmetry of the latter is
\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}
; see
(
Bhardwaj:2021pfz
, (3.12))
. With the CS levels turned on, their ’t Hooft anomalies are characterised by (
2.15
) and (
2.16
).

3.1
Cases that satisfy the ATT condition

We consider theories (
3.1
) with CS levels satisfying (
2.6
).

3.1.1
Special case of
(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})=(-k,2k,2k)

This theory is equivalent to the
\USp(2)_{-k}\times\mathrm{Spin}(4)_{2k}
gauge theory with two copies of bifundamental half-hypermultiplets in the representation
[\mathbf{2};\mathbf{4}]
:

\begin{split}\hbox to146.33pt{\vbox to62.11pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 73.73167pt\lower-31.05254pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}

\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\end{split}

(3.5)

It is indeed closely related to the ABJ theory
Aharony:2008gk
, described by the
\USp(2)_{-k}\times\mathrm{O}(4)_{2k}
gauge theory with the same matter content. We can start from the
\USp(2)_{-k}\times\mathrm{SO}(4)_{2k}
variant of the theory: gauging the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
zero-form charge conjugation symmetry associated with the
\mathrm{SO}(4)
gauge group leads to the original ABJ theory, whereas gauging the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
zero-form magnetic symmetry leads to theory (
3.5
). This type of arguments was used to studied variants of ABJM
Aharony:2008ug
and ABJ theories in
Tachikawa:2019dvq
;
Bergman:2020ifi
;
Beratto:2021xmn
;
Mekareeya:2022spm
. The index of the
\USp(2)_{-k}\times\mathrm{SO}(4)_{2k}
variant is

\begin{split}&{\cal I}_{\USp(2)_{-k}\times\mathrm{SO}(4)_{2k}}(\zeta,a;x)\\
&=\frac{1}{8}\sum_{(\mathfrak{m}_{1},\mathfrak{m}_{2})\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\,\sum_{\mathfrak{n}\in\mathbb{Z}}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{2}\oint\frac{dv_{j}}{2\pi iv_{j}}\,v_{j}^{2k\mathfrak{m}_{j}}\right)\zeta^{\mathfrak{m}_{1}+\mathfrak{m}_{2}}\oint\frac{du}{2\pi iu}\,u^{-2k\mathfrak{n}}\times\\
&\quad{\cal Z}^{\mathrm{SO}(4)}_{\text{vec}}(v_{1},v_{2};\mathfrak{m}_{1},\mathfrak{m}_{2};x){\cal Z}^{\USp(2)}_{\text{vec}}(u;\mathfrak{n};x)\times\\
&\quad\prod_{i=1}^{2}\,\prod_{s_{1},s_{2}=\pm 1}{\cal Z}^{1/2}_{\chi}\left(v_{i}^{s_{1}}u^{s_{2}}a;s_{1}\mathfrak{m}_{i}+{s_{2}}\mathfrak{n};x\right){\cal Z}^{1/2}_{\chi}\left(v_{i}^{s_{1}}u^{s_{2}}a^{-1};s_{1}\mathfrak{m}_{i}+{s_{2}}\mathfrak{n};x\right)~.\end{split}

(3.6)

where
\zeta
(with
\zeta^{2}=1
) denotes the fugacity for the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
zero-form magnetic symmetry and we have set the fugacity
\chi
for the charge conjugation symmetry to
1
. The index of (
3.5
) can be obtained by gauging the magnetic symmetry as follows:

\begin{split}{\cal I}_{(\ref{USp2Spin4ABJ})}(a;x)=\frac{1}{2}\left[{\cal I}_{\USp(2)_{-k}\times\mathrm{SO}(4)_{2k}}(\zeta=1,a;x)+{\cal I}_{\USp(2)_{-k}\times\mathrm{SO}(4)_{2k}}(\zeta=-1,a;x)\right]~.\end{split}

(3.7)

Let us first consider the index of the
\USp(2)_{-k}\times\mathrm{SO}(4)_{2k}
variant of the ABJ theory, or equivalently the
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{-k}\times[\mathrm{SU}(2)_{2k}\times\mathrm{SU}(2)_{2k}]/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
theory, where a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
one-form symmetry of (
3.1
) is gauged. We tabulate the results, up to order
x^{4}
, below.

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.82}{
\begin{tabular}[]{|c|c|}\hline\cr CS levels&Index (\ref{indUSp2SO4ABJ})\\
$(-k,2k,2k)$&\\
\hline\cr$k=1$&${1}+{\left[{1}+{\zeta}{\chi_{[2]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)\right]}{x}+{\left[{\left({2}+{\zeta}\right)}{{\chi_{[4]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}+{2}-{{\chi_{[2]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}\right]}{x^{2}}+$\\
&${\left\{{{\left({1}+{2}{\zeta}\right)}{{\chi_{[6]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}-{\left[{\zeta}{{\chi_{[4]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}+{\left({3}+{2}{\zeta}\right)}{{\chi_{[2]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}+{\zeta}\right]}}\right\}}{x^{3}}+$\\
&$\left\{{\left({3}+{2}{\zeta}\right)}{{\chi_{[8]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}+{3}{\left({1}+{\zeta}\right)}{{\chi_{[2]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}+{1}+{2}{\zeta}\right.-$\\
&$\left.{\left[{\left({2}+{\zeta}\right)}{{\chi_{[6]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}+{\left({1}+{\zeta}\right)}{{\chi_{[4]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}\right]}\right\}{x^{4}}+{\ldots}$\\
\hline\cr$k=2$&${1}+{x}+{\left[{\left({1}+{\zeta}\right)}{{\chi_{[4]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}+{2}-{{\chi_{[2]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}\right]}{x^{2}}+$\\
&${\left[{\zeta}{{\chi_{[6]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}+{2}-{\left({2}+{\zeta}\right)}{{\chi_{[2]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}\right]}{x^{3}}+$\\
&$\left\{{\left({2}+{\zeta}\right)}{{\chi_{[8]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}+{2}-{\left[{\left({1}+{\zeta}\right)}{{\chi_{[6]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}+{\zeta}{{\chi_{[4]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}+{\zeta}{{\chi_{[2]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}\right]}\right\}{x^{4}}+{\ldots}$\\
\hline\cr$k=3$&${1}+{x}+{\left[{{\chi_{[4]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}+{2}-{{\chi_{[2]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}\right]}{x^{2}}+{\left[{{\zeta}{{\chi_{[6]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}+{2}-{2}{{\chi_{[2]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}}\right]}{x^{3}}+$\\
&$\left\{{\left({1}+{\zeta}\right)}{{\chi_{[8]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}+{2}-{\left[{{\chi_{[6]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}+{\zeta}{{\chi_{[4]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}\right]}\right\}{x^{4}}+{\ldots}$\\
\hline\cr$k=4$&${1}+{x}+{\left[{{\chi_{[4]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}+{2}-{{\chi_{[2]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}\right]}{x^{2}}+{2}{\left[{{1}-{{\chi_{[2]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}}\right]}{x^{3}}+$\\
&$\left\{{\left({1}+{\zeta}\right)}{{\chi_{[8]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}+{2}-{{\chi_{[6]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}\right\}{x^{4}}+{\ldots}$\\
\hline\cr\end{tabular}}\end{split}

(3.8)

For
k\geq 5
, the terms with fugacity
\zeta
appear at a higher order than
x^{4}
. The index of the case of
k=1
was studied in
(
Beratto:2021xmn
, (3.80))
, where it was pointed out that the
\USp(2)_{-1}\times\mathrm{SO}(4)_{2}
ABJ theory is dual to another variant of the ABJ theory, namely the
[\U(3)_{4}\times\U(1)_{-4}]/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
gauge theory with two bifundamental hypermultiplets, whose IR SCFT has
enhanced
{\cal N}=6
supersymmetry
. The indices for the cases of
k\geq 2
indicate that supersymmetry gets
enhanced to
{\cal N}=5
. This can be seen as follows. All of such indices have the coefficient of
x
equal to
1
indicating that there is one
{\cal N}=3
flavour current, but since there is the term
-{{\chi_{[2]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}=-(a^{2}+1+a^{-2})
at order
x^{2}
, the term
-1
corresponds to the
{\cal N}=3
flavour current and the terms
-a^{2}
and
-a^{-2}
correspond to the
{\cal N}=3
extra SUSY-currents, rendering supersymmetry enhancement from
{\cal N}=3
to
{\cal N}=5
.

Now we report the index of theory (
3.1
) with CS levels
(-k,2k,2k)
, or equivalently (
3.5
), given by
{\cal I}_{(\ref{USp2Spin4ABJ})}(a;x)
up to order
x^{4}
below.

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.89}{
\begin{tabular}[]{|c|c|}\hline\cr CS levels&Index ${\cal I}_{(\ref{USp2Spin4ABJ})}(a;x)$\\
$(-k,2k,2k)$&\\
\hline\cr$k=1$&${1}+{x}+{\left\{{2}{\left[{\chi_{[4]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)+{1}\right]}-{{\chi_{[2]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}\right\}}{x^{2}}+{\left[{{{\chi_{[6]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}-{3}{{\chi_{[2]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}}\right]}{x^{3}}+$\\
&${\left\{{3}{\left[{{\chi_{[8]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}+{{\chi_{[2]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}\right]}+{1}-{\left[{2}{{\chi_{[6]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}+{{\chi_{[4]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}\right]}\right\}}{x^{4}}+{\ldots}$\\
\hline\cr$k=2$&${1}+{x}+{\left[{{\chi_{[4]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}+{2}-{{\chi_{[2]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}\right]}{x^{2}}+{2}{\left[{{1}-{{\chi_{[2]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}}\right]}{x^{3}}+$\\
&${\left\{{{2}{\left[{{\chi_{[8]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}+{1}\right]}-{{\chi_{[6]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}}\right\}}{x^{4}}+{\ldots}$\\
\hline\cr$k\geq 3$&${1}+{x}+{\left[{{\chi_{[4]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}+{2}-{{\chi_{[2]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}\right]}{x^{2}}+$\\
&${2}{\left[{{1}-{{\chi_{[2]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}}\right]}{x^{3}}+{\left[{{{\chi_{[8]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}+{2}-{{\chi_{[6]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}}\right]}{x^{4}}+\ldots$\\
\hline\cr\end{tabular}}\end{split}

(3.9)

For
{k}\geq{3}
the indices differ from each other at a higher order than
x^{4}
. By the same reasoning as before, the indices indicate that the IR SCFT has enhanced
{\cal N}=5
supersymmetry.

The ’t Hooft anomalies of the one-form symmetry of these theories are as presented in (
2.30
). For
k
odd, theory (
3.1
) with CS levels
(-k,2k,2k)
or equivalently (
3.5
) flows to an interacting
{\cal N}=5
SCFT with a non-anomalous
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
one-form symmetry, along with the TQFT
{\cal A}^{2,1}
.
17
17

17

For
k
odd, we can infer from (
2.31
) that the
\USp(2)_{-k}\times\mathrm{SO}(4)_{2k}
theory does not admit a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
quotient. This means that, for
k
odd, the non-anomalous one-form symmetry of the
\USp(2)_{-k}\times\mathrm{Spin}(4)_{2k}
is
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
, which we can gauge in order to obtain the
\USp(2)_{-k}\times\mathrm{SO}(4)_{2k}
theory, and there is no further
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
one-form symmetry that we can gauge in the latter.
For
k
even, the theory flows to an interacting
{\cal N}=5
SCFT that has a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{2}
one-form symmetry and there is no decoupled topological sector.

The above indices receive the contributions of the gauge invariant dressed monopole operators, whose properties are similar to that discussed around (
2.28
). Explicitly, the bare monopole operator with magnetic fluxes
(m_{1},m_{2},m_{3})
has dimension

\begin{split}\Delta(m_{1},m_{2},m_{3})=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{s_{1},s_{2},s_{3}=\pm 1}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{3}s_{i}m_{i}\right|-\sum_{i=1}^{3}2|m_{i}|~,\end{split}

(3.10)

is neutral under the flavour symmetry and it carries charge
2k_{i}m_{i}
under the Cartan subalgebra of each
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{i}
gauge factor.

We can compute the Coulomb branch and Higgs branch limits of the index as in (
2.37
). We find that they are equal, as expected for SCFTs with
{\cal N}\geq 5
supersymmetry. In particular, we have

\begin{split}&\text{Higgs limit $(\ref{twoT2})_{(-k,2k,2k)}$}=\text{Coulomb limit $(\ref{twoT2})_{(-k,2k,2k)}$}\\
&=\mathop{\rm PE}\left[t^{2}+t^{2k}+t^{2k+1}-t^{4k+2}\right]~,\quad\text{with $t=h$ or $c$}~.\end{split}

(3.11)

This is the Hilbert series of
\mathbb{C}^{2}/\widehat{D}_{2k+2}
, indicating that the Higgs and Coulomb branches are isomorphic to this singularity. The generators of the Higgs branch are

\begin{split}w=\mathop{\rm tr}(\mu^{(1)\,2})~,\quad v=X_{(2,1,1)}\left(Q^{(1)}\right)^{4k}~,\quad u=X_{(2,1,1)}\left(Q^{(1)}\right)^{4k-4}\mu^{(1)}_{1}\mu^{(1)}_{2}\mu^{(1)}_{3}~,\end{split}

(3.12)

satisfying the relation

\begin{split}u^{2}+v^{2}w=w^{2k+1}~.\end{split}

(3.13)

The generators of the Coulomb branch can be obtained simply by replacing the superscript
(1)
by
(2)
. Note that the generators
u
and
v
are the bare monopole operator
X_{(2,1,1)}
, whose dimension is zero, dressed by appropriate chiral fields from each copy of
T_{2}
such that the combinations become gauge invariant. Indeed, from (
3.9
), we see that the dressed monopoles that are related to
v
contribute the term
\chi^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}_{[4k]}(a)x^{2k}
to the index.

We can also examine the
\USp(2)_{-k}\times\mathrm{SO}(4)_{2k}
version of the ABJ theory, or equivalently
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{-k}\times[\mathrm{SU}(2)_{2k}\times\mathrm{SU}(2)_{2k}]/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
, which comes from gauging a non-anomalous
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
subgroup of the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{2}
one-form symmetry of the aforementioned theory. In this case, we have

\begin{split}&\text{Higgs limit $(\ref{twoT2})_{(-k,2k,2k)}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[1]}$}=\text{Coulomb limit $(\ref{twoT2})_{(-k,2k,2k)}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{[1]}$}\\
&=\mathop{\rm PE}\left[t^{2}+t^{k}+t^{k+1}-t^{2k+2}\right]~,\quad\text{with $t=h$ or $c$}~.\end{split}

(3.14)

This is the Hilbert series of
\mathbb{C}^{2}/\widehat{D}_{k+2}
. The generators of the Higgs branch are

\begin{split}w=\mathop{\rm tr}(\mu^{(1)\,2})~,\quad v=X_{\left(1,\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}\right)}\left(Q^{(1)}\right)^{2k}~,\quad u=X_{\left(1,\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}\right)}\left(Q^{(1)}\right)^{2k-4}\mu^{(1)}_{1}\mu^{(1)}_{2}\mu^{(1)}_{3}~,\end{split}

(3.15)

satisfying the relation

\begin{split}u^{2}+v^{2}w=w^{k+1}~.\end{split}

(3.16)

Again, the Coulomb branch generators can be obtained by replacing the superscript
(1)
by
(2)
. From (
3.8
), we see that the dressed monopoles that are related to
v
contribute the term
\zeta\chi^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}_{[2k]}(a)x^{k}
to the index. This explains why, when
k\geq 5
, the contribution from the dressed monopole operators appears at a higher order than
x^{4}
. In particular, for
k=1
, these operators are related to
{\cal N}=3
flavour currents that are necessary for the enhanced
{\cal N}=6
supersymmetry in the IR.

3.1.2
General results for
(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})=k(\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q},-\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{r},-\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{r})
with
\mathfrak{r}=\mathfrak{p}+\mathfrak{q}

We consider the CS levels (
2.44
) for the theories formed by gauging two copies of the
T_{2}
theory. The information about the one-form symmetries and their ’t Hooft anomalies are as tabulated in (
2.46
).

Let us now consider the index (
3.4
). The contribution from flux
(m_{1},m_{2},m_{3})=(0,0,0)
, up to order
x^{4}
, reads

\begin{split}&{1}+{x}+{\left[{{\chi_{[4]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}+{2}-{{\chi_{[2]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}\right]}{x^{2}}+\\
&{2}{\left[{{1}-{{\chi_{[2]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}}\right]}{x^{3}}+{\left[{{{\chi_{[8]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}+{2}-{{\chi_{[6]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}}\right]}{x^{4}}+\ldots~.\end{split}

(3.17)

The term at order
x
is the contribution from

\begin{split}\epsilon^{\alpha_{1}\alpha^{\prime}_{1}}\epsilon^{\alpha_{2}\alpha^{\prime}_{2}}\epsilon^{\alpha_{3}\alpha^{\prime}_{3}}Q^{(1)}_{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}\alpha_{3}}Q^{(2)}_{\alpha^{\prime}_{1}\alpha^{\prime}_{2}\alpha^{\prime}_{3}}~.\end{split}

(3.18)

This is the moment map operator associated with the
\U(1)

{\cal N}=3
flavour symmetry current. The marginal operators contributing the positive terms, namely
{{\chi_{[4]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)}+{2}
, at order
x^{2}
are

\begin{split}Q^{((I_{1})}Q^{(I_{2})}Q^{(I_{3})}Q^{(I_{4}))_{S}}~,\quad\mathop{\rm tr}(\mu^{(1)}_{1}\mu^{(2)}_{1})~,\quad\mathop{\rm tr}(\mu^{(1)}_{2}\mu^{(2)}_{2})~,\end{split}

(3.19)

where
\mathop{\rm tr}(\mu^{(1)}_{3}\mu^{(2)}_{3})
can be written as a linear combination of the latter two due to the
F
-terms. Also, in the first quantities, the contractions of gauge indices, which we have suppressed, are done in such a way that
I_{1},\ldots I_{4}
are completely symmetric; the latter is denoted by
()_{S}
. Note also that the first quantities contain
\mathop{\rm tr}(\mu^{(1)\,2})
and
\mathop{\rm tr}(\mu^{(2)\,2})
.

There is also a contribution from eight gauge fluxes
(m_{1},m_{2},m_{3})=(\pm\mathfrak{r},\pm\mathfrak{q},\pm\mathfrak{p})
corresponding to the gauge-invariant dressed monopole operators. According to the discussion around (
3.10
), the bare monopoles associated with these fluxes have dimension
0
, are neutral under the flavour symmetry, and the charges under the Cartan subalgebra of each
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{i}
are
2k\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{r}(\pm 1,\mp 1,\mp 1)
. They have to be dressed with
2k\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{r}
chiral fields from each copy of
T_{2}
, or to form gauge invariant quantities. The gauge invariant dressed monopole operators therefore contribute to the index as
\chi^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}_{[2k\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{r}]}(a)x^{k\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{r}}
. If
k\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{r}
is sufficiently large, the index at sufficiently low order does not get affected by these operators. In any case, using the same argument as above, we see from (
3.17
) that the SCFT in the IR has enhanced
{\cal N}=5
supersymmetry, regardless of the contribution of the dressed monopole operators.

3.1.3
Higgs and Coulomb branches

For the theories discussed in Section (
3.1.2
), the Higgs and Coulomb branch limits of the index are both equal to the Hilbert series of
\mathbb{C}^{2}/\widehat{D}_{K+2}
, namely

\begin{split}\mathop{\rm PE}\left[t^{2}+t^{K}+t^{K+1}-t^{2K+2}\right]~,\quad K=\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{r}k~,~\text{$t=h$ or $c$}~.\end{split}

(3.20)

The generators of the Higgs or Coulomb branch are

\begin{split}w=\mathop{\rm tr}(\mu^{(I)\,2})~,\quad v=X_{(\mathfrak{r},\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p})}(Q^{(I)})^{2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{r}k}~,\quad u=X_{(\mathfrak{r},\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p})}(Q^{(I)})^{2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{r}k-4}\mu^{(I)}_{1}\mu^{(I)}_{2}\mu^{(I)}_{3}~,\end{split}

(3.21)

with
I=1
or
2
. They satisfy the relation

\begin{split}u^{2}+v^{2}w=w^{K+1}~.\end{split}

(3.22)

3.2
Cases that do not satisfy the ATT condition

From the effective superpotential (
3.3
), we see that the first term can be viewed as an
{\cal N}=3
preserving exactly marginal deformation of the
{\cal N}=5
theory whose superpotential contains only the second term. The index of the latter, excluding the contribution from the dressed monopoles, is given by (
3.17
), which indeed indicates
{\cal N}=5
supersymmetry. The aforementioned exactly marginal deformation explicitly breaks the
\mathfrak{su}(2)_{a}
flavour symmetry, corresponding to the term
-{\chi_{[2]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)x^{2}
in the index, to its Cartan subalgebra. The latter can be seen from the term
+x
of the index which indicates that the
{\cal N}=3
flavour symmetry is
\U(1)
.
18
18

18

To see the action of this symmetry, we view the moment maps
\mu^{(I)}
as
2\times 2
symmetric matrices
\mu^{(I)}=\begin{pmatrix}a^{(I)}&b^{(I)}\\
b^{(I)}&c^{(I)}\end{pmatrix}
. Under this
\U(1)
symmetry, the elements
a^{(I)}
and
c^{(I)}
carry charges
+1
and
-1
respectively, whereas
b^{(I)}
carry charge
0
. Indeed, the terms
\mathop{\rm tr}(\mu^{(I)\,2})=2a^{(I)}c^{(I)}-2b^{(I)\,2}
and
\mathop{\rm tr}(\mu^{(1)}\mu^{(2)})=a^{(2)}c^{(1)}+a^{(1)}c^{(2)}-2b^{(1)}b^{(2)}
in the superpotential are neutral under this flavour symmetry, as it should be.
. From the index (
3.17
) with
a=1
, we have

\begin{split}1+x+(5-1)x^{2}-4x^{3}+4x^{4}+\ldots~,\end{split}

(3.23)

where we see that 7 marginal operators as listed in (
3.19
) get reduced to 5 due to the two
F
-term relations coming from the non-vanishing first term in the superpotential (
3.3
). Indeed this is due to the terms
a^{2}
and
a^{-2}
in the term
-{\chi_{[2]}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}}\left({a}\right)x^{2}
being set to
1
. In general, we expect that the cases that do not satisfy the ATT condition have
{\cal N}=3
supersymmetry. Although we have not taken into account the contributions of the dressed monopole operators, we do not expect the latter to make supersymmetry enhanced.

Nevertheless, the dressed monopole operators can make the
flavour symmetry
enhanced. An example is the case of CS levels
(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})=(-1,1,1)
, whose index is

\begin{split}1+3x+(9-3)x^{2}-7x^{3}+16x^{4}+\ldots~.\end{split}

(3.24)

The contributions to
3x
come from (
3.18
), along with the dressed monopole operators
X_{(1,1,0)}Q^{(1)}Q^{(2)}
and
X_{(1,0,1)}Q^{(1)}Q^{(2)}
. We propose that these form a triplet of the
enhanced
\mathrm{SO}(3)
flavour symmetry
which is indeed the moment map of this symmetry. For convenience, let denote by
b
the fugacity of this
\mathrm{SO}(3)
flavour symmetry, and so the term
3x
can be rewritten as
\chi^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}_{[2]}(b)x
. Note that the square of such dressed monopole operators contribute to the index at order
x^{2}
; therefore, the term
(9-3)x^{2}
can be rewritten as
\left[\chi^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}_{[4]}(b)+4-\chi^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}_{[2]}(b)\right]x^{2}
. The term
b^{0}=1
in
\chi^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}_{[4]}(b)
, together with
+4
, accounts for the 5 marginal operators, as mentioned above; the remaining positive terms are marginal operators that are dressed monopole operators. We do not see any contribution of the
{\cal N}=3
extra SUSY-current, and so we conclude that the theory has
{\cal N}=3
supersymmetry.

Gluing with
T(\mathrm{SU}(2))
theories

\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\end{split}

(3.25)

where
S
stands for the
T(\mathrm{SU}(2))
theory. The
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{i}
global symmetry left
T_{2}
theory is diagonally gauged with the
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{C}
symmetry of the
i
-th copy of the
T(SU(2))
theory with CS level
k_{i}^{(1)}
(with
i=1,2,3
), and the
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{i}
global symmetry right
T_{2}
theory is diagonally gauged with the
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{H}
symmetry of the
i
-th copy of the
T(SU(2))
theory with CS level
k_{i}^{(2)}
. The analogue of the ATT condition (
2.65
) is the following
Assel:2022row
:

\begin{split}\frac{q_{1}}{p_{1}}+\frac{q_{2}}{p_{2}}+\frac{q_{3}}{p_{3}}=0~,\qquad\frac{q^{\prime}_{1}}{p_{1}}+\frac{q^{\prime}_{2}}{p_{2}}+\frac{q^{\prime}_{3}}{p_{3}}=0~,\end{split}

(3.26)

where

\begin{split}\frac{p_{i}}{q_{i}}=k^{(1)}_{i}-\frac{1}{k^{(2)}_{i}}~,\qquad\frac{p_{i}}{q^{\prime}_{i}}=k^{(2)}_{i}-\frac{1}{k^{(1)}_{i}}~.\end{split}

(3.27)

For convenience, we will also refer to (
3.26
) as the ATT conditions. The index of these theories is given by the following expression:

\begin{split}&{\cal I}_{(\ref{twoT2withTSU2})}(a,n_{a};x)\\
&=\left(\frac{1}{8}\prod_{i=1}^{3}\oint\frac{dz_{i}}{2\pi iz_{i}}\right)\sum_{(m_{1},\cdots,m_{3})\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\left(\frac{1}{8}\prod_{i=1}^{3}\oint\frac{df_{i}}{2\pi if_{i}}\right)\sum_{(\widehat{m}_{1},\cdots,\widehat{m}_{3})\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\times\\
&\quad\ \left(\prod_{i=1}^{3}z_{i}^{2k^{(1)}_{i}m_{i}}{\cal Z}^{\mathrm{SU}(2)}_{\text{vec}}(z_{i};m_{i};x)\right)\left(\prod_{i=1}^{3}f_{i}^{2k^{(2)}_{i}\widehat{m}_{i}}{\cal Z}^{\mathrm{SU}(2)}_{\text{vec}}(f_{i};\widehat{m}_{i};x)\right)\times\\
&\quad\ \prod_{s_{1},s_{2},s_{3}=\pm 1}{\cal Z}^{1/2}_{\chi}(z_{1}^{s_{1}}z_{2}^{s_{2}}z_{3}^{s_{3}}a;s_{1}m_{1}+s_{2}m_{2}+s_{3}m_{3}+n_{a};x)\times\\
&\quad\ \prod_{s_{1},s_{2},s_{3}=\pm 1}{\cal Z}^{1/2}_{\chi}(z_{1}^{s_{1}}z_{2}^{s_{2}}z_{3}^{s_{3}}a^{-1};s_{1}m_{1}+s_{2}m_{2}+s_{3}m_{3}-n_{a};x)\times\\
&\quad\ \prod_{i=1}^{3}{\cal I}_{T(\mathrm{SU}(2))}(z_{i},m_{i}|f_{i},\widehat{m}_{i}|a,n_{a};x)~.\end{split}

(3.28)

As before, when both conditions in (
3.26
) are satisfied, there is a flavour symmetry that assigns charge
+1
to the chiral fields of the first copy of the
T_{2}
theory, charge
-2
to the Coulomb branch moment map of
T(\mathrm{SU}(2))
,
+2
to the Higgs branch moment map of
T(\mathrm{SU}(2))
, and charge
-1
to the chiral fields of the second copy of the
T_{2}
theory. If one of the conditions in (
3.26
) is not satisfied, this symmetry is explicitly broken and we should set
a=1
and
n_{a}=0
in the above expression.

’t Hooft anomalies of the one-form symmetries

Similarly to (
2.69
), here we have six
\mathrm{SU}(2)
gauge groups but
\mathbb{Z}^{4}_{2}
one-form symmetry due to the screening effect of the matter of two copies of the
T_{2}
theory. The ’t Hooft anomalies are characterised by

\begin{split}&\pi\int_{{\cal M}_{4}}\left[\sum_{r=1}^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{3}k^{(r)}_{i}\frac{{\cal P}(B_{i}^{(r)})}{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{3}B_{i}^{(1)}\cup B_{i}^{(2)}\right]\\
&\quad\text{with}~\sum_{i=1}^{3}B_{i}^{(1)}=\sum_{i=1}^{3}B_{i}^{(2)}=0~,\end{split}

(3.29)

where
B_{i}^{(r)}
is the two-form background field associated with the
\mathrm{SU}(2)
gauge group with CS level
k_{i}^{(r)}
, with
i=1,2,3
and
r=1,2
. The last constraint comes from (
2.12
).

(3.30)

with various CS levels. We find the following results:

•

When both conditions in (
3.26
) are satisfied, IR SCFT always has enhanced
{\cal N}=4
supersymmetry.

•

When either of the conditions (
3.26
) is not satisfied, we generally do not find an indication of supersymmetry enhancement from the index.

We report the indices as follows.

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.74}{
\begin{tabular}[]{|c|c|c|c|c|c|c|}\hline\cr$k^{(1)}_{1}$&$k^{(2)}_{1}$&$\frac{p_{1}}{q_{1}},\frac{p_{1}}{q^{\prime}_{1}}$&$k_{2}$&$k_{3}$&(\ref{ATT3})&Index\\
\hline\cr$2$&$2$&$\frac{3}{2}$, $\frac{3}{2}$&$-1$&$3$&✓\,\, ✓&$1+(a^{4}+a^{-4}+2-1)x^{2}-(2a^{2}+2a^{-2})x^{3}+$\\
&&&&&&$(2a^{8}+2a^{-8}-1)x^{4}+\ldots$\\
\hline\cr$2$&$2$&$\frac{3}{2}$, $\frac{3}{2}$&$-3$&$-3$&✓\,\, ✓&$1+(a^{4}+a^{-4}+2-1)x^{2}+(a^{6}-2a^{2}-2a^{-2}+a^{-6})x^{3}+$\\
&&&&&&$(2a^{8}-a^{4}-2-a^{-4}+2a^{-8})x^{4}+\ldots$\\
\hline\cr\hline\cr$1$&$-1$&$2$, $-2$&1&1&✗ \,\, ✗&$1+0x+3x^{2}-4x^{3}+3x^{4}+\ldots$\\
\hline\cr$-2$&$-1$&$-1,-\frac{1}{2}$&$1$&$1$&✗\, \, ✓&$1+x+4x^{2}-4x^{3}+13x^{4}+\ldots$\\
\hline\cr$-2$&$-1$&$-1,-\frac{1}{2}$&$2$&$2$&✓ \,\, ✗&$1+0x+4x^{2}-3x^{3}+3x^{4}+\ldots$\\
\hline\cr\end{tabular}}\end{split}

(3.31)

Let us first consider the cases in which both conditions in (
3.26
) are satisfied. The contribution of the gauge fluxes that are all zero is

\begin{split}1+0x+(a^{4}+a^{-4}+2-1)x^{2}-(2a^{2}+2a^{-2})x^{3}+(a^{8}+a^{-8})x^{4}+\ldots~.\end{split}

(3.32)

Comparing with the first two rows in the above table, we see that the contributions of the higher monopole fluxes generally appear at higher order of
x
. Since the coefficient of
x
vanishes, there is no
{\cal N}=3
flavour current. The current associated with the
\U(1)_{a}
flavour symmetry, contributing the term
-x^{2}
, acts as the
{\cal N}=3
extra-SUSY current. The latter implies that the IR SCFTs have
enhanced
{\cal N}=4
supersymmetry
. Due to the vanishing coefficient of
x
, the index does not satisfy the sufficient conditions to have
{\cal N}\geq 5
supersymmetry
Evtikhiev:2017heo
. The marginal operators contributing the terms
a^{4}+a^{-4}+2
at order
x^{2}
are

\begin{split}a^{\pm 4}:&\quad\epsilon^{a_{1}b_{1}}\epsilon^{c_{1}d_{1}}\epsilon^{a_{2}c_{2}}\epsilon^{b_{2}d_{2}}\epsilon^{a_{3}c_{3}}\epsilon^{b_{3}d_{3}}Q^{(I)}_{a_{1}a_{2}a_{3}}Q^{(I)}_{b_{1}b_{2}b_{3}}Q^{(I)}_{c_{1}c_{2}c_{3}}Q^{(I)}_{d_{1}d_{2}d_{3}}~,\quad I=1,2~,\\
1:&\quad\epsilon^{a_{1}b_{1}}\epsilon^{\widehat{c}_{1}\widehat{d}_{1}}\epsilon^{a_{2}c_{2}}\epsilon^{b_{2}d_{2}}\epsilon^{a_{3}c_{3}}\epsilon^{b_{3}d_{3}}Q^{(1)}_{a_{1}a_{2}a_{3}}Q^{(1)}_{b_{1}b_{2}b_{3}}Q^{(2)}_{\widehat{c}_{1}c_{2}c_{3}}Q^{(2)}_{\widehat{d}_{1}d_{2}d_{3}}~,\\
1:&\quad\epsilon^{a_{1}b_{1}}\epsilon^{\widehat{c}_{1}\widehat{d}_{1}}\epsilon^{a_{2}c_{2}}\epsilon^{b_{2}d_{2}}\epsilon^{a_{3}d_{3}}\epsilon^{b_{3}c_{3}}Q^{(1)}_{a_{1}a_{2}a_{3}}Q^{(1)}_{b_{1}b_{2}b_{3}}Q^{(2)}_{\widehat{c}_{1}c_{2}c_{3}}Q^{(2)}_{\widehat{d}_{1}d_{2}d_{3}}~.\end{split}

(3.33)

The other gauge invariant combinations with
R
-charge
2
are related to these combinations by the identities of the epsilon tensors or the
F
-term conditions.

However, in the cases in which one or both of the conditions (
3.26
) is not satisfied, the
\U(1)_{a}
flavour symmetry is explicitly broken, and so the marginal operators listed in (
3.33
) may be related to each other by the
F
-terms (
cf.
Sections
2.3
and
3.2
). In these cases, we do not see clear evidence of supersymmetry enhancement from the index.

4
Theories with
T_{3}
building blocks

We now consider theories whose building blocks are the 3d
T_{3}
theory. Let us start by summarising the important information of the
T(\mathrm{SU}(3))
and
T_{3}
theories. The
T(\mathrm{SU}(3))
theory has an
\mathrm{SU}(3)_{H}\times\mathrm{SU}(3)_{C}
global symmetry with a mixed anomaly characterised by

\begin{split}\frac{2\pi}{3}\int_{{\cal M}_{4}}w_{2}^{H}\cup w_{2}^{C}~,\end{split}

(4.1)

where
w_{2}^{H/C}
is the second Stiefel-Whitney class which measures the obstruction to lifting the
(\mathrm{SU}(3)/\mathbb{Z}_{3})_{H/C}
bundle to the
SU(3)_{H/C}
bundle. The 3d
{\cal N}=4

T_{3}
theory can then be constructed by gauging the diagonal
\mathrm{SU}(3)/\mathbb{Z}_{3}
subgroup of the
\mathrm{SU}(3)^{3}_{H}
symmetry coming from three copies of the
T(\mathrm{SU}(3))
theory. Note that the
\mathrm{SU}(3)^{3}_{C}
manifest flavour symmetry of the
T_{3}
theory gets enhanced to
E_{6}
in the IR. The moment map in the adjoint representation of
E_{6}
can be decomposed into fields in representations of the
\mathrm{SU}(3)^{3}
maximal subgroup as follows:

\begin{split}\begin{tabular}[]{ccccccc}$\mathbf{78}$&\,$\rightarrow$&$[\mathbf{8};\mathbf{1};\mathbf{1}]$ \,\, $\oplus$&$[\mathbf{1};\mathbf{8};\mathbf{1}]$\,\, $\oplus$&$[\mathbf{1};\mathbf{1};\mathbf{8}]$ \,\, $\oplus$&$[\mathbf{3};\mathbf{3};\mathbf{3}]$\,\, $\oplus$&$[\overline{\mathbf{3}};\overline{\mathbf{3}};\overline{\mathbf{3}}]$\\
&&$X^{i_{1}}_{j_{1}}$&$Y^{i_{2}}_{j_{2}}$&$Z^{i_{3}}_{j_{3}}$&${\cal Q}^{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}$&$\widetilde{{\cal Q}}_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}$\\
\end{tabular}~.\end{split}

(4.2)

They satisfy the following relations (see
(
Maruyoshi:2013hja
, Section 2.2)
and
(
Tachikawa:2015bga
, Section 5.3)
):

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.9}{$\begin{split}&\mathrm{tr}_{1}(X^{2})=\mathrm{tr}_{2}(Y^{2})=\mathrm{tr}_{3}(Z^{2})\equiv\mathbb{M}_{2}~,\\
&\mathrm{tr}_{1}(X^{3})=\mathrm{tr}_{2}(Y^{3})=\mathrm{tr}_{3}(Z^{3})\equiv\mathbb{M}_{3}~,\\
&X^{i_{1}}_{j_{1}}{\cal Q}^{j_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}=Y^{i_{2}}_{j_{2}}{\cal Q}^{i_{1}j_{2}i_{3}}=Z^{i_{3}}_{j_{3}}{\cal Q}^{i_{1}i_{2}j_{3}}~,\\
&X^{j_{1}}_{i_{1}}\widetilde{{\cal Q}}_{j_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}=Y^{j_{2}}_{i_{2}}\widetilde{{\cal Q}}_{i_{1}j_{2}i_{3}}=Z^{j_{3}}_{i_{3}}\widetilde{{\cal Q}}_{i_{1}i_{2}j_{3}}~,\\
&{\cal Q}^{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}\widetilde{{\cal Q}}_{j_{1}j_{2}i_{3}}=\sum_{l=0}^{3}v_{l}\sum_{m=0}^{2-l}(X^{2-l-m})^{i_{1}}_{j_{1}}(Y^{m})^{i_{2}}_{j_{2}}~,\quad v_{0}=1~,\,\,v_{1}=0~,\,\,(X^{0})^{i}_{j}=(Y^{0})^{i}_{j}=\delta^{i}_{j}~,\\
&\frac{1}{2}{\cal Q}^{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}{\cal Q}^{j_{1}j_{2}j_{3}}\epsilon_{i_{2}j_{2}k_{2}}\epsilon_{i_{3}j_{3}k_{3}}=\widetilde{{\cal Q}}_{k_{1}k_{2}k_{3}}\delta^{i_{1}}_{p_{1}}X^{j_{1}}_{q_{1}}\epsilon^{p_{1}q_{1}k_{1}}~,\\
&\frac{1}{2}\widetilde{{\cal Q}}_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}\widetilde{{\cal Q}}_{j_{1}j_{2}j_{3}}\epsilon^{i_{2}j_{2}k_{2}}\epsilon^{i_{3}j_{3}k_{3}}={\cal Q}^{k_{1}k_{2}k_{3}}\delta_{i_{1}}^{p_{1}}X_{j_{1}}^{q_{1}}\epsilon_{p_{1}q_{1}k_{1}}~,\end{split}$}\end{split}

(4.3)

where
\mathrm{tr}_{i}
denotes the trace over the fundamental representation of the
\mathrm{SU}(3)_{i}
symmetry (with
i=1,2,3
) of the
T_{3}
theory.

4.1
One
T_{3}
building block

The theory of our interest is obtained by gauging each
\mathrm{SU}(3)_{C}
factor of the
\mathrm{SU}(3)^{3}_{C}
symmetry with CS levels
k_{1}
,
k_{2}
and
k_{3}
. Similarly to (
2.1
), we denote this theory by

\begin{split}\hbox to68.79pt{\vbox to70.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 8.55322pt\lower-31.78577pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{26.58781pt}{4.533pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{65}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\end{split}

(4.4)

’t Hooft anomalies of the one-form symmetry

Since the faithful manifest flavour symmetry of the
T_{3}
theory is
\mathrm{SU}(3)^{3}/(\mathbb{Z}_{3}\times\mathbb{Z}_{3})

(
Bhardwaj:2021ojs
, (4.40))
, it follows that theory (
4.4
) has a
\mathbb{Z}_{3}^{2}
one-form symmetry. The ’t Hooft anomaly of the one-form symmetry in theory (
4.4
) with CS levels
(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})
is characterised by the 4d anomaly theory whose action is
Benini:2017dus

\begin{split}\frac{2\pi}{3}\int_{{\cal M}_{4}}\sum_{i=1}^{3}k_{i}\frac{{\cal P}(w_{i}^{(2)})}{2}~,\quad\text{with \,\, $\sum_{i=1}^{3}w_{i}^{(2)}=0$}~,\end{split}

(4.5)

where
w_{i}^{(2)}
is the two-form background field for the
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
one-form symmetry arising from the
\mathrm{SU}(3)_{i}
gauge group of (
4.4
).

Superconformal indices

The index of the
T(\mathrm{SU}(3))
theory is given by

\begin{split}&{\cal I}_{T(\mathrm{SU}(3))}(\bm{w},\bm{n}|\bm{f},\bm{m}|a,n_{a};x)\\
&=\frac{1}{2!}\sum_{h\in\mathbb{Z}+\bm{\epsilon}(\bm{m})}\oint\frac{du}{2\pi iu}w_{1}^{h}u^{n_{1}}{\cal Z}_{\chi}^{1}(a^{-2};-2n_{a};x)\times\\
&\qquad\sum_{l_{1},l_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}+\bm{\epsilon}(\bm{m})}\oint\left(\prod_{\alpha=1}^{2}\frac{dz_{\alpha}}{2\pi iz_{\alpha}}z^{n_{2}}_{\alpha}\right)w_{2}^{l_{1}+l_{2}}\prod_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{2}{\cal Z}_{\chi}^{1}(z_{\alpha}z^{-1}_{\beta}a^{-2};l_{\alpha}-l_{\beta}-2n_{a};x)\times\\
&\qquad\ {\cal Z}^{\U(2)}_{\text{vec}}(\{z_{1},z_{2}\};\{l_{1},l_{2}\};x)\times\prod_{\alpha=1}^{2}\prod_{s=\pm 1}{\cal Z}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\chi}\left(a(uz^{-1}_{\alpha})^{s};s(h-l_{\alpha})+n_{a};x\right)\times\\
&\qquad\ \prod_{i=1}^{3}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{2}\prod_{s=\pm 1}{\cal Z}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\chi}\left(a(z_{\alpha}f^{-1}_{i})^{s};s(l_{\alpha}-m_{i})+n_{a};x\right)~,\end{split}

(4.6)

where
(\bm{w},\bm{n})
,
(\bm{f},\bm{m})
and
(a,n_{a})
are
(fugacities, background fluxes)
for the topological, flavour, and axial symmetries respectively. In the above,
\bm{\epsilon}(\bm{m})
denotes the fractional part of the background fluxes
m_{i}
. The
\U(N)
vector multiplet contribution is

\begin{split}{\cal Z}^{\U(N)}_{\text{vec}}\left(\bm{z};\bm{n};x\right)&=x^{-\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq N}|n_{i}-n_{j}|}\prod_{1\leq i\neq j\leq N}(1-(-1)^{n_{i}-n_{j}}x^{|n_{i}-n_{j}|}z_{i}z_{j}^{-1})~.\end{split}

(4.7)

Note that the index of the
T(\mathrm{SU}(3))
theory is invariant under the mirror symmetry in the following sense:

\begin{split}&\widehat{{\cal I}}_{T(\mathrm{SU}(3))}(\{w_{1};w_{2}\},\{n_{1},n_{2}\}|\{f_{1},f_{2}\},\{m_{1},m_{2}\}|a,n_{a};x)\\
&=\widehat{{\cal I}}_{T(\mathrm{SU}(3))}(\{f_{1},f_{2}\},\{m_{1};m_{2}\}|\{w_{1},w_{2}\},\{n_{1},n_{2}\}|a^{-1},-n_{a};x)~,\end{split}

(4.8)

where we have defined

\begin{split}&\widehat{{\cal I}}_{T(\mathrm{SU}(3))}(\{w_{1},w_{2}\},\{n_{1};n_{2}\}|\{f_{1},f_{2}\},\{m_{1},m_{2}\}|a,n_{a};x)\\
&:={\cal I}_{T(\mathrm{SU}(3))}(\{w_{1}w_{2}^{-1},w_{1}^{-2}w_{2}^{-1}\},\{n_{1}-n_{2},-2n_{1}-n_{2}\}|\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\{f_{1},f_{2},f_{1}^{-1}f_{2}^{-1}\},\{m_{1},m_{2},-m_{1}-m_{2}\}|a,n_{a};x)~.\end{split}

(4.9)

The index of the
T_{3}
theory is therefore

\begin{split}&{\cal I}_{T_{3}}(\bm{w}^{(1)},\bm{n}^{(1)}|\bm{w}^{(2)},\bm{n}^{(2)}|\bm{w}^{(3)},\bm{n}^{(3)}|a,n_{a};x)\\
&=\frac{1}{3!}\sum_{r=0}^{2}\,\,\sum_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}+\frac{r}{3}}\,\,\oint\left(\prod_{\alpha=1}^{2}\frac{df_{\alpha}}{2\pi if_{\alpha}}\right){\cal Z}^{\mathrm{SU}(3)}_{\text{vec}}\left(\bm{f};\bm{m};x\right)\times\\
&\qquad\ \prod_{I=1}^{3}\widehat{{\cal I}}_{T(\mathrm{SU}(3))}(\bm{w}^{(I)},\bm{n}^{(I)}|\bm{f},\bm{m}|a,n_{a};x)~,\end{split}

(4.10)

where the
\mathrm{SU}(3)
vector multiplet contribution is given by

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.95}{$\begin{split}{\cal Z}^{\mathrm{SU}(3)}_{\text{vec}}\left(\{z_{1},z_{2}\};\{n_{1},n_{2}\};x\right)&={\cal Z}^{\U(3)}_{\text{vec}}\left(\{z_{1},z_{2},z_{1}^{-1}z_{2}^{-1}\};\{n_{1},n_{2},-n_{1}-n_{2}\};x\right)~.\end{split}$}\end{split}

(4.11)

The index of the theory of our interest (
4.4
) is then

\begin{split}&{\cal I}_{(\ref{singleT3})}(a,n_{a};x)\\
&=\frac{1}{(3!)^{3}}\prod_{i=1}^{3}\sum_{n^{(i)}_{1},n^{(i)}_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\oint\frac{dw_{1}^{(i)}}{2\pi w_{1}^{(i)}}\frac{dw_{2}^{(i)}}{2\pi w_{2}^{(i)}}\,\,(w_{1}^{(i)})^{k_{i}(2n^{(i)}_{1}+n^{(i)}_{2})}(w_{2}^{(i)})^{k_{i}(n^{(i)}_{1}+2n^{(i)}_{2})}\times\\
&\quad\ \left[\prod_{i=1}^{3}{\cal Z}^{\mathrm{SU}(3)}_{\text{vec}}\left(\bm{w}^{(i)};\bm{n}^{(i)};x\right)\right]\times{\cal I}_{T_{3}}(\bm{w}^{(1)},\bm{n}^{(1)}|\bm{w}^{(2)},\bm{n}^{(2)}|\bm{w}^{(3)},\bm{n}^{(3)}|a,n_{a};x)~.\end{split}

(4.12)

As before, if the ATT condition (
2.6
) is not satisfied, we set
a=1
and
n_{a}=0
.

Due to the technicality of the computation, let us discuss the results only in certain cases. We first focus on the theories that satisfy the ATT condition and we set
n_{a}=0
. The contribution from the fluxes
n^{(i)}_{1},n^{(i)}_{2}=0
for all
i=1,2,3
is

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.99}{$1+0x+(a^{4}-1)x^{2}+(a^{6}-a^{2}+a^{-2}+a^{-6})x^{3}+(a^{8}-1-a^{-4}+a^{-8})x^{4}+\ldots~.$}\end{split}

(4.13)

This index holds when the CS levels are sufficiently high so that the contributions from non-zero fluxes
n^{(i)}_{1},n^{(i)}_{2}
appear at a higher order.
19
19

19

For example, the index for theory (
4.4
) with CS levels
(-1,2,2)
is
1+0x+(a^{4}-1)x^{2}+(a^{6}-a^{2}+a^{-2}-3a^{-6})x^{3}+(a^{8}-1-5a^{-4}+2a^{-8})x^{4}+\ldots
. On the other hand, for CS levels
(-3,6,6)
, the index up to order
x^{4}
is given by (
4.13
).
The term
0x
implies that there is no
{\cal N}=3
flavour symmetry current. Therefore, the term
-x^{2}
indicates that there is one extra SUSY-current, implying that the IR SCFT has enhanced
{\cal N}=4
supersymmetry. The term
a^{4}x^{2}
corresponds to the marginal operator
\mathbb{M}_{2}
, defined in (
4.3
). When the ATT condition is not satisfied, we set
a=1
in (
4.13
). In this case, the index does not provide any evidence for supersymmetry enhancement.

4.2
Two
T_{3}
building blocks

Similarly to (
3.1
), we can couple two copies of the
T_{3}
theory together by gauging a diagonal subgroup of the two
\mathrm{SU}(3)_{i}
flavour symmetries (with
i=1,2,3
), belonging to different copies of the
T_{3}
theories, with CS levels
k_{i}
.

\begin{split}\hbox to102.47pt{\vbox to46.18pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 8.55322pt\lower-18.48053pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{38.99405pt}{-10.43999pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{65}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\end{split}

(4.14)

This theory has a
\mathbb{Z}_{3}^{2}
one-form symmetry, whose ’t Hooft anomaly is given by (
4.5
). The index of this theory is given by

\begin{split}&{\cal I}_{(\ref{twoT3})}(a,n_{a};x)\\
&=\frac{1}{(3!)^{3}}\prod_{i=1}^{3}\sum_{n^{(i)}_{1},n^{(i)}_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\oint\frac{dw_{1}^{(i)}}{2\pi w_{1}^{(i)}}\frac{dw_{2}^{(i)}}{2\pi w_{2}^{(i)}}\,\,(w_{1}^{(i)})^{k_{i}(2n^{(i)}_{1}+n^{(i)}_{2})}(w_{2}^{(i)})^{k_{i}(n^{(i)}_{1}+2n^{(i)}_{2})}\times\\
&\quad\left[\prod_{i=1}^{3}{\cal Z}^{\mathrm{SU}(3)}_{\text{vec}}\left(\bm{w}^{(i)};\bm{n}^{(i)};x\right)\right]\prod_{s=\pm 1}{\cal I}_{T_{3}}(\bm{w}^{(1)},\bm{n}^{(1)}|\bm{w}^{(2)},\bm{n}^{(2)}|\bm{w}^{(3)},\bm{n}^{(3)}|a^{s},sn_{a};x)~.\end{split}

(4.15)

Once again, the fugacity
a
and background magnetic flux
n_{a}
for the flavour symmetry should be set to
1
and
0
respectively if the ATT condition (
2.6
) is not satisfied.

Due to the technicality of the computation, we set
n_{a}=0
and we report only the contributions of the zero gauge fluxes
n_{1}^{(i)},n_{2}^{(i)}=0
:

\begin{split}{1}+0x+{\left({a^{4}}+{a^{-4}}+{4}-1\right)}{x^{2}}+{2}{\left({a^{6}}+{a^{-6}}\right)}{x^{3}}+{\left[{2}{\left({a^{8}}+{a^{-8}}\right)}+{3}\right]}{x^{4}}+{\ldots}~.\end{split}

(4.16)

Note that this is the index of theory (
4.14
) with sufficiently large CS levels
k_{1,2,3}
. As before, the term
0x
indicates that there is no
{\cal N}=3
flavour symmetry current, and so the term
-1x^{2}
indicates that there is one extra SUSY-current. The IR SCFT indeed has enhanced
{\cal N}=4
supersymmetry. The positive terms at order
x^{2}
correspond to the following marginal operators:

\begin{split}{a^{\pm 4}}:&\quad\mathbb{M}^{(1)}_{2}~,\,\,\mathbb{M}^{(2)}_{2}~,\\
{4}:&\quad{\mathrm{tr}_{1}{\left({X^{(1)}}{X^{(2)}}\right)}}~,\ {\mathrm{tr}_{2}{\left({Y^{(1)}}{Y^{(2)}}\right)}}~,\ {\mathrm{tr}_{3}{\left({Z^{(1)}}{Z^{(2)}}\right)}}\ {\text{and}}\ {{\cal Q}^{{(1)}{i_{1}}{i_{2}}{i_{3}}}}{\widetilde{{\cal Q}}^{(2)}_{{i_{1}}{i_{2}}{i_{3}}}}~,\end{split}

(4.17)

where we have used the same notation as in (
4.3
) with an extra superscript
(I)
such that
I=1,2
to denote the
I
-th copy of the
T_{3}
theory.

(A.2)

Let us compute the index of our theory, which is given by

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.83}{$\begin{split}&\mathcal{I}_{(\ref{T24copies})}\left({a},{n_{a}};{x}\right)\\
&={\frac{1}{64}}\,\,\sum_{(m_{1},\ldots,m_{6})\in{\mathbb{Z}^{6}}}\,\,\oint{\left(\prod_{{b}={1}}^{6}{\frac{dz_{b}}{{2}{\pi}{i}{z_{b}}}}\right)}\times\\
&\qquad{z_{1}^{-{2}{k}{m_{1}}}}{z_{2}^{{4}{k}{m_{2}}}}{z_{3}^{{4}{k}{m_{3}}}}{z_{4}^{-{2}{k}{m_{4}}}}{z_{5}^{{4}{k}{m_{5}}}}{z_{6}^{{4}{k}{m_{6}}}}\prod_{{b}={1}}^{6}{{\mathcal{Z}_{\text{vec}}^{SU(2)}}\left({z_{b}};{m_{b}};x\right)}\prod_{{s_{1}},{s_{2}},{s_{3}}={\pm{1}}}\times\\
&\left[{{\mathcal{Z}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\chi}}\left({z_{1}^{s_{1}}}{z_{2}^{s_{2}}}{z_{3}^{s_{3}}}{a};{{s_{1}}{m_{1}}}+{{s_{2}}{m_{2}}}+{{s_{3}}{m_{3}}}+{n_{a}};x\right)}{{\mathcal{Z}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\chi}}\left({z_{2}^{s_{1}}}{z_{3}^{s_{2}}}{z_{4}^{s_{3}}}{a^{-1}};{{s_{1}}{m_{2}}}+{{s_{2}}{m_{3}}}+{{s_{3}}{m_{4}}}-{n_{a}};x\right)}\times\right.\\
&\left.{{\mathcal{Z}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\chi}}\left({z_{4}^{s_{1}}}{z_{5}^{s_{2}}}{z_{6}^{s_{3}}}{a};{{s_{1}}{m_{4}}}+{{s_{2}}{m_{5}}}+{{s_{3}}{m_{6}}}+{n_{a}};x\right)}{{\mathcal{Z}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\chi}}\left({z_{5}^{s_{1}}}{z_{6}^{s_{2}}}{z_{1}^{s_{3}}}{a^{-1}};{{s_{1}}{m_{5}}}+{{s_{2}}{m_{6}}}+{{s_{3}}{m_{1}}}-{n_{a}};x\right)}\right]~,\end{split}$}\end{split}

(A.3)

where
a
and
n_{a}
are the fugacity and background magnetic flux for the flavour symmetry that assigns charge
+1
to the chiral fields of theories
T_{2}^{(1)}
and
T_{2}^{(3)}
and
-1
to those of theories
T_{2}^{(2)}
and
T_{2}^{(4)}
. For simplicity, we will set
{n_{a}}={0}
upon computing the series expansion of the index.

On the other hand, the index of theory (
A.2
) can be obtained starting from the one of the
{\USp({2})_{-k}}\times{\mathrm{SO}({4})_{{2}{k}}}\times{\USp({2})_{-k}}\times{\mathrm{SO}({4})_{{2}{k}}}

(A.4)

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.9}{$\begin{split}&\mathcal{I}_{(\ref{T24copiesb})}{\left({\zeta_{1}},{\zeta_{2}},{a};x\right)}\\
&={\frac{1}{64}}\,\,\sum_{\mathfrak{m}_{1},\ldots,\mathfrak{m}_{4},\mathfrak{n}_{1},\mathfrak{n}_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\,\,\oint{\left(\prod_{{b}={1}}^{4}{\frac{dv_{b}}{{2}{\pi}{i}{v_{b}}}}{v_{b}^{{2}{k}{\mathfrak{m}_{b}}}}\right)}{\zeta_{1}^{{\mathfrak{m}_{1}}+{\mathfrak{m}_{2}}}}{\zeta_{2}^{{\mathfrak{m}_{3}}+{\mathfrak{m}_{4}}}}\oint{\left(\prod_{{b}={1}}^{2}{\frac{du_{b}}{{2}{\pi}{i}{u_{b}}}}{u_{b}^{-{2}{k}{\mathfrak{n}_{b}}}}\right)}{\times}\\
&\qquad{{\mathcal{Z}_{\text{vec}}^{\mathrm{SO}(4)}}\left({v_{1}},{v_{2}};{\mathfrak{m}_{1}},{\mathfrak{m}_{2}};x\right)}{{\mathcal{Z}_{\text{vec}}^{\USp(2)}}\left({u_{1}};{\mathfrak{n_{1}}};x\right)}{\times}\\
&\qquad{{\mathcal{Z}_{\text{vec}}^{\mathrm{SO}(4)}}\left({v_{3}},{v_{4}};{\mathfrak{m}_{3}},{\mathfrak{m}_{4}};x\right)}{{\mathcal{Z}_{\text{vec}}^{\USp(2)}}\left({u_{2}};{\mathfrak{n_{2}}};x\right)}{\times}\\
&{\prod_{{b}={1}}^{2}\ \prod_{{s_{1}},{s_{2}}={\pm{1}}}{{\mathcal{Z}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\chi}}\left({v_{b}^{s_{1}}}{u_{1}^{s_{2}}}{a};{{s_{1}}{\mathfrak{m}_{b}}}+{{s_{2}}{\mathfrak{n}_{1}}};x\right)}{{\mathcal{Z}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\chi}}\left({v_{b}^{s_{1}}}{u_{2}^{s_{2}}}{a^{-1}};{{s_{1}}{\mathfrak{m}_{b}}}+{{s_{2}}{\mathfrak{n}_{2}}};x\right)}}{\times}\\
&{\prod_{{b}={3}}^{4}\ \prod_{{s_{1}},{s_{2}}={\pm{1}}}{{\mathcal{Z}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\chi}}\left({v_{b}^{s_{1}}}{u_{1}^{s_{2}}}{a^{-1}};{{s_{1}}{\mathfrak{m}_{b}}}+{{s_{2}}{\mathfrak{n}_{1}}};x\right)}{{\mathcal{Z}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\chi}}\left({v_{b}^{s_{1}}}{u_{2}^{s_{2}}}{a};{{s_{1}}{\mathfrak{m}_{b}}}+{{s_{2}}{\mathfrak{n}_{2}}};x\right)}}~,\end{split}$}\end{split}

(A.5)

where
\zeta_{1}
and
\zeta_{2}
are the fugacities associated with the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
zero-form magnetic symmetries of the two
\mathrm{SO}(4)_{{2}{k}}
gauge factors, and the fugacities for the zero-form charge conjugation symmetries are set to unity. In order to obtain the index of the circular quiver (
A.2
) with
\mathrm{Spin}
gauge groups, we have to sum over both
{\zeta_{1}}=\pm{1}
and
{\zeta_{2}}=\pm{1}
and divide by four:

\begin{split}\mathcal{I}_{(\ref{T24copiesa})}\left({a};{x}\right)={\frac{1}{4}}\sum_{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}=\pm 1}\mathcal{I}_{(\ref{T24copiesb})}\left({\zeta_{1}},{\zeta_{2}},{a};{x}\right).\end{split}

(A.6)

It is easy to check that the indices (
A.3
) and (
A.6
) are equal if we perform the following map between the gauge fugacities and magnetic fluxes of the two theories:

\begin{array}[]{lll}{z_{1}}={u_{1}}~,&\qquad{z_{2}^{2}}={v_{1}}{v_{2}}~,&\qquad{z_{3}^{2}}={v_{1}}{v_{2}^{-1}}~,\\
{z_{4}}={u_{2}}~,&\qquad{z_{5}^{2}}={v_{3}}{v_{4}}~,&\qquad{z_{6}^{2}}={v_{3}}{v_{4}^{-1}}~,\\
{m_{1}}={\mathfrak{n}_{1}}~,&\qquad{2}{m_{2}}={\mathfrak{m}_{1}}+{\mathfrak{m}_{2}}~,&\qquad{2}{m_{3}}={\mathfrak{m}_{1}}-{\mathfrak{m}_{2}}~,\\
{m_{4}}={\mathfrak{n}_{2}}~,&\qquad{2}{m_{5}}={\mathfrak{m}_{3}}+{\mathfrak{m}_{4}}~,&\qquad{2}{m_{6}}={\mathfrak{m}_{3}}-{\mathfrak{m}_{4}}~.\end{array}

(A.7)

It can now be checked that the IR SCFT in question exhibits
{\mathcal{N}}={4}
supersymmetry enhancement, as expected from the general prescription described in the main text, by computing the index as a series expansion in the variable
x
. We report the results up to order
x^{4}
for various values of
k
:

\begin{split}&{\mathcal{I}_{(\ref{T24copies})}}{\left({a};{x}\right)}={\mathcal{I}_{(\ref{T24copiesa})}}{\left({a};{x}\right)}\\
k=1:\quad&={1}+{\left({2}{a^{4}}+{2}{a^{-4}}+{3}\right)}{x^{2}}-{4}{\left({a^{2}}+{a^{-2}}\right)}{x^{3}}+\\
&\qquad{\left({6}{a^{8}}+{3}{a^{4}}+{3}{a^{-4}}+{6}{a^{-8}}+{7}\right)}{x^{4}}+\ldots~,\\
k=2:\quad&={1}+{\left({2}{a^{4}}+{2}{a^{-4}}+{3}\right)}{x^{2}}-{4}{\left({a^{2}}+{a^{-2}}\right)}{x^{3}}+\\
&\qquad{\left({3}{a^{8}}+{2}{a^{4}}+{2}{a^{-4}}+{3}{a^{-8}}+{4}\right)}{x^{4}}+\ldots~.\end{split}

(A.8)

Note that for
{k}\geq{2}
the indices differ at a higher order than
x^{4}
in the expansion. We notice that the coefficient of
x
vanishes, meaning that there is no
{\mathcal{N}}={3}
flavour current. The coefficient of
x^{2}
should be written as

{2}{a^{4}}+{2}{a^{-4}}+{4}-{1}~,

(A.9)

where the term
-1
is the contribution of the
{\mathcal{N}}={3}
extra SUSY-current that leads to
{\mathcal{N}}={4}
supersymmetry enhancement. The marginal operators, contributing the positive terms
{2}{a^{4}}+{2}{a^{-4}}+{4}
, can be listed as follows:

\begin{split}{2}{a^{4}}:&\ \mathop{\rm tr}(\mu^{(1)\,2})\ \text{and}\ \mathop{\rm tr}(\mu^{(3)\,2})~,\\
{2}{a^{-4}}:&\ \mathop{\rm tr}(\mu^{(2)\,2})\ \text{and}\ \mathop{\rm tr}(\mu^{(4)\,2})~,\\
{4}:&\ \text{gauge invariant combinations of two chiral fields ${Q^{(I)}_{{i_{I}}{j_{I}}{k_{I}}}}$ of $T_{2}^{(I)}$}\\
&\ \text{and two chiral fields ${Q^{(J)}_{{i_{J}}{j_{J}}{k_{J}}}}$ of $T_{2}^{(J)}$, with ${I}={1},{3}$ and ${J}={2},{4}$}~.\end{split}

(A.10)

We can gauge the one-form symmetry of the theory (
A.1
) to obtain the
{SU(2)_{-k}}\times{\left[{SU(2)_{{2}{k}}}\times{SU(2)_{{2}{k}}}\right]/{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}}\times{SU(2)_{-k}}\times{\left[{SU(2)_{{2}{k}}}\times{SU(2)_{{2}{k}}}\right]/{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}}
gauge theory, as depicted below.

}\end{split}

(A.11)

It turns out that this theory is equivalent to the circular quiver theory (
A.4
). The index of theory (
A.11
) can be obtained from (
A.3
) by replacing the summation as

\begin{split}\sum_{(m_{1},\ldots,m_{6})\in{\mathbb{Z}^{6}}}\quad\longrightarrow\quad{\sum_{{p}={0}}^{1}}{{\zeta_{1}}^{p}}\,\,{\sum_{{p^{\prime}}={0}}^{1}}{{\zeta_{2}}^{p^{\prime}}}{\sum_{{{m_{1}},{m_{4}}}\in{\mathbb{Z}^{2}}}}\ \ {\sum_{{{m_{2}},{m_{3}}}\in{\left(\mathbb{Z}+{\frac{p}{2}}\right)^{2}}}}\,\,\,{\sum_{{{m_{5}},{m_{6}}}\in{\left(\mathbb{Z}+{\frac{p^{\prime}}{2}}\right)^{2}}}}~,\end{split}

(A.12)

where
\zeta_{1}
and
\zeta_{2}
are the fugacities of the zero-form symmetry of (
A.11
). The result of this procedure is equal to the index (
A.5
).
20
20

20

Indeed, by taking into account the map (
A.7
),
\zeta_{1}
and
\zeta_{2}
are such that

{{\zeta_{1}}^{{\mathfrak{m}_{1}}\pm{\mathfrak{m}_{2}}}}=\begin{cases}{1}&{{\mathfrak{m}_{1}}\pm{\mathfrak{m}_{2}}}\in{\mathbb{Z}_{\text{even}}}\longleftrightarrow{{m_{2}},{m_{3}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\longleftrightarrow{{p}={0}}\\
{\zeta_{1}}&{{\mathfrak{m}_{1}}\pm{\mathfrak{m}_{2}}}\in{\mathbb{Z}_{\text{odd}}}\longleftrightarrow{{m_{2}},{m_{3}}\in{\frac{\mathbb{Z}_{\text{odd}}}{2}}}\longleftrightarrow{{p}={1}}\end{cases}~,

{{\zeta_{2}}^{{\mathfrak{m}_{3}}\pm{\mathfrak{m}_{4}}}}=\begin{cases}{1}&{{\mathfrak{m}_{3}}\pm{\mathfrak{m}_{4}}}\in{\mathbb{Z}_{\text{even}}}\longleftrightarrow{{m_{5}},{m_{6}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\longleftrightarrow{{p^{\prime}}={0}}\\
{\zeta_{2}}&{{\mathfrak{m}_{3}}\pm{\mathfrak{m}_{4}}}\in{\mathbb{Z}_{\text{odd}}}\longleftrightarrow{{m_{5}},{m_{6}}\in{\frac{\mathbb{Z}_{\text{odd}}}{2}}}\longleftrightarrow{{p^{\prime}}={1}}\end{cases}~.

Let us report the results for various values of
k
up to order
x^{4}
.
For
{k}={1}
, the expansion of the index up to order
x^{4}
reads

\begin{split}&\mathcal{I}_{(\ref{T24copiesmodZ2})}{\left({\zeta_{1}},{\zeta_{2}},a;{x}\right)}=\mathcal{I}_{(\ref{T24copiesb})}{\left({\zeta_{1}},{\zeta_{2}},{a};{x}\right)}\\
k=1:\quad&={1}+{\left[{\left({2}+{\zeta_{1}}+{\zeta_{2}}+{\zeta_{1}\zeta_{2}}\right)}{\left({a^{4}}+{a^{-4}}\right)}+{3}+{\zeta_{1}}+{\zeta_{2}}+{\zeta_{1}\zeta_{2}}\right]}{x^{2}}+\\
&\quad\left[{\left({\zeta_{1}}+{\zeta_{2}}+{2}{\zeta_{1}\zeta_{2}}\right)}{a^{6}}+{\left(-{4}-{\zeta_{1}}-{\zeta_{2}}+{2}{\zeta_{1}\zeta_{2}}\right)}{\left({a^{2}}+{a^{-2}}\right)}+\right.\\
&\quad\left.{\left({\zeta_{1}}+{\zeta_{2}}+{2}{\zeta_{1}\zeta_{2}}\right)}{a^{-6}}\right]{x^{3}}+\\
&\quad\left[{\left({6}+{3}{\zeta_{1}}+{3}{\zeta_{2}}+{3}{\zeta_{1}\zeta_{2}}\right)}{a^{8}}+{\left({3}-{\zeta_{1}}-{\zeta_{2}}-{3}{\zeta_{1}\zeta_{2}}\right)}{\left({a^{4}}+{a^{-4}}\right)}\right.+\\
&\quad\left.{\left({6}+{3}{\zeta_{1}}+{3}{\zeta_{2}}+{3}{\zeta_{1}\zeta_{2}}\right)}{a^{-8}}+{7}-{3}{\zeta_{1}\zeta_{2}}\right]{x^{4}}+\ldots~,\end{split}

(A.13)

For
{k}={2},{3}
we get

\begin{split}&\mathcal{I}_{(\ref{T24copiesmodZ2})}{\left({\zeta_{1}},{\zeta_{2}},a;{x}\right)}=\mathcal{I}_{(\ref{T24copiesb})}{\left({\zeta_{1}},{\zeta_{2}},{a};{x}\right)}\\
k=2:\quad&={1}+{\left({2}{a^{4}}+{2}{a^{-4}}+{3}\right)}{x^{2}}+\\
&\quad\left[{\left({\zeta_{1}}+{\zeta_{2}}\right)}{a^{6}}-{4}{\left({a^{2}}+{a^{-2}}\right)}+{\left({\zeta_{1}}+{\zeta_{2}}\right)}{a^{-6}}\right]{x^{3}}+\\
&\quad\left[{\left({3}+{\zeta_{1}}+{\zeta_{2}}+{\zeta_{1}\zeta_{2}}\right)}{a^{8}}+{\left({2}+{\zeta_{1}\zeta_{2}}\right)}{\left({a^{4}}+{a^{-4}}\right)}+\right.\\
&\quad\left.{\left({3}+{\zeta_{1}}+{\zeta_{2}}+{\zeta_{1}\zeta_{2}}\right)}{a^{-8}}+{4}+{\zeta_{1}\zeta_{2}}\right]{x^{4}}+\ldots~,\\
k=3:\quad&={1}+{\left({2}{a^{4}}+{2}{a^{-4}}+{3}\right)}{x^{2}}-{4}{\left({a^{2}}+{a^{-2}}\right)}{x^{3}}+\\
&\quad\left[{\left({3}+{\zeta_{1}}+{\zeta_{2}}\right)}{a^{8}}+{2}{\left({a^{4}}+{a^{-4}}\right)}+{\left({3}+{\zeta_{1}}+{\zeta_{2}}\right)}{a^{-8}}+{4}\right]{x^{4}}+\ldots~.\end{split}

(A.14)

For
{k}\geq 4
the fugacities
{\zeta_{1}}
and
{\zeta_{2}}
appear at a higher order than
x^{4}
.

From the expansion of the indices, it is clear that we have two independent
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
fugacities
\zeta_{1}
and
\zeta_{2}
satisfying
{{\zeta_{1}}^{2}}={\zeta_{2}^{2}}={1}
. Hence, the elements of
\left\{{1},{\zeta_{1}},{\zeta_{2}},{\zeta_{1}\zeta_{2}}\right\}
corresponding to the possible choices of
{p}=\left\{{0},{1}\right\}
and
{p^{\prime}}=\left\{{0},{1}\right\}
in (
A.12
) indicate the presence of the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}
zero-form symmetry in theory (
A.4
) = (
A.11
), and hence the
{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\times{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}
one-form symmetry of theory (
A.1
) = (
A.2
).

From the point of view of the
{\USp({2})_{-k}}\times{\mathrm{SO}({4})_{{2}{k}}}\times{\USp({2})_{-k}}\times{\mathrm{SO}({4})_{{2}{k}}}
circular quiver theory (
A.4
), it is expected by a similar argument that leads to (
2.32
) that there is a non-anomalous
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
one-form symmetry for
k
even. In principle, one can further gauge this one-form symmetry, generalising what we did in (
2.36
).

Appendix B
Mixed gauge/zero-form monopole operators

In this appendix, we examine the mixed gauge/zero-form monopole operators with
fractional
magnetic flux for both the Cartan subalgebra of the gauge group and the
\U(1)_{a}
flavour symmetry group
Bhardwaj:2022dyt
;
Bhardwaj:2023zix
for theories (
2.1
) with one
T_{2}
building block such that the ATT condition (
2.6
) is satisfied. We adopt the same method as described in
Mekareeya:2022spm
(see also
Sacchi:2023omn
) which relies on the supersymmetric index. In particular, we take
n_{a}=1/2
in (
2.7
) and examine the contribution of the gauge fluxes
m_{1,2,3}
satisfying the Dirac quantisation condition, namely
\pm m_{1}\pm m_{2}\pm m_{3}+n_{a}\in\mathbb{Z}
, i.e.

\begin{split}\sum_{i}m_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}+\frac{1}{2}~.\end{split}

(B.1)

Let us report the result for various theories with one
T_{2}
building block below.

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.75}{
\begin{tabular}[]{|c|c|c|}\hline\cr CS levels&Gauge fluxes&Contribution to the index (\ref{indexsingleT2})\\
&$(m_{1},m_{2},m_{3})$&\\
\hline\cr$(-1,2,2)$&$\left(\frac{1}{2},0,0\right)$&0\\
&$\left(0,\frac{1}{2},0\right)$&$X_{2}\equiv\left(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2a^{2}}\right)x+\left(\frac{a^{4}}{2}-1-\frac{1}{a^{2}}-\frac{1}{2a^{4}}\right)x^{5}+O(x^{7})$\\
&$\left(0,0,\frac{1}{2}\right)$&$X_{2}$\\
&$\left(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}\right)$&0\\
\hline\cr\hline\cr$(-2,4,4)$&$\left(\frac{1}{2},0,0\right)$&$X_{-2}\equiv\frac{x}{2a^{2}}+\left(\frac{a^{2}}{2}-\frac{1}{a^{2}}-\frac{1}{2a^{4}}\right)x^{5}+O(x^{7})$\\
&$\left(0,\frac{1}{2},0\right)$&$X_{4}\equiv\left(\frac{a^{2}}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\right)x^{2}+\left(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2a^{2}}\right)x^{4}+\left(\frac{a^{6}}{2}-a^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\right)x^{6}+O(x^{7})$\\
&$\left(0,0,\frac{1}{2}\right)$&$X_{4}$\\
&$\left(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}\right)$&$\left(\frac{a^{3}}{8}+\frac{a}{4}+\frac{1}{8a}\right)x^{3/2}+\left(\frac{1}{8a}+\frac{1}{8a^{3}}\right)x^{7/2}+O(x^{11/2})$\\
&&$=\frac{1}{4}\left[\{x^{1/2}(a^{5}+a^{3})+\ldots\}X_{-2}+\{x^{-1/2}a^{-1}+\ldots\}X_{4}\right]$\\
\hline\cr\hline\cr$(-2,3,6)$&$\left(\frac{1}{2},0,0\right)$&$X_{-2}$\\
&$\left(0,\frac{1}{2},0\right)$&$0$\\
&$\left(0,0,\frac{1}{2}\right)$&$X_{6}\equiv\left(\frac{a^{4}}{2}+\frac{a^{2}}{2}\right)x^{3}+\left(\frac{a^{2}}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\right)x^{5}+\left(\frac{a^{8}}{2}-a^{4}+\frac{1}{2}\right)x^{7}+O(x^{9})$\\
&$\left(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}\right)$&$0$\\
\hline\cr\hline\cr$(-4,6,12)$&$\left(\frac{1}{2},0,0\right)$&$X_{-4}\equiv\left(\frac{1}{2a^{2}}+\frac{1}{2}\right)x^{4}+\left(\frac{1}{2a^{4}}+\frac{1}{2a^{6}}\right)x^{6}+\left(\frac{1}{2a^{6}}+\frac{a^{4}}{2}-\frac{3}{2a^{2}}-\frac{3}{2}\right)x^{8}+O(x^{10})$\\
&$\left(0,\frac{1}{2},0\right)$&$X_{6}$\\
&$\left(0,0,\frac{1}{2}\right)$&$X_{12}\equiv\left(\frac{a^{10}}{2}+\frac{a^{8}}{2}\right)x^{6}+\left(\frac{a^{8}}{2}+\frac{a^{6}}{2}\right)x^{8}+\left(\frac{a^{14}}{2}-a^{10}+\frac{a^{6}}{2}\right)x^{10}+O(x^{12})$\\
&$\left(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}\right)$&$\left(\frac{a^{11}}{8}+\frac{a^{9}}{4}+\frac{a^{7}}{8}\right)x^{11/2}+\left(\frac{a^{9}}{8}+\frac{a^{7}}{4}+\frac{a^{5}}{8}\right)x^{15/2}+O(x^{19/2})$\\
\hline\cr\end{tabular}}\end{split}

(B.2)

From the above results, we observe that whenever all CS levels are even, the contribution of the gauge fluxes
(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})
contains odd powers of
a
. This corresponds to a different quantisation in comparison to, for example (
2.27
), where only even powers of
a
appear. Note, however, that this is not the case if there exists an odd CS level. The presence of such mixed gauge/zero-form monopole operators, when all CS levels are even, potentially implies a mixed anomaly between a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
one-form symmetry and the
\U(1)_{a}
symmetry
Bhardwaj:2022dyt
;
Bhardwaj:2023zix
(see also
Mekareeya:2022spm
;
Sacchi:2023omn
). In our case, such a mixed anomaly is characterised by the 4d anomaly action
\pi\int_{{\cal M}_{4}}w^{(2)}\cup c_{1}^{a}
, where
w^{(2)}
is the two-form background field for a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
subgroup of the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}
non-anomalous one-form symmetry and
c_{1}^{a}
is the first Chern class of a background
\U(1)_{a}
flavour symmetry bundle.
21
21

21

Let us compare the above interpretation with respect to (
2.36
). Recall that in the latter we gauge the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}
one-form symmetry of theory (
2.1
) with CS levels
(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})=(-4,8,8)
and obtain the dual
\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}
zero-form symmetry associated with the fugacities
\zeta
and
s
. Observe that only even powers of
a
appear in (
2.36
), and we do not see any indication of the mixed anomaly between a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
subgroup the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}
zero-form symmetry and the
\U(1)_{a}
zero-form symmetry. A natural question that arises is whether this is in contradiction with the above interpretation regarding the mixed anomaly between the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
one-form symmetry and the
\U(1)_{a}
flavour symmetry. (Note also that the quantisation condition (
B.1
) with
n_{a}=1/2
is different from (
2.35
) with
n_{a}=0
.) We leave this for a future investigation.

A similar analysis can be performed for theories (
3.1
) with two
T_{2}
building blocks such that the ATT condition (
2.6
) is satisfied. The only difference is that in the present case the faithful flavour symmetry group is
\mathrm{SO}(3)_{a}
. This can be seen from the discussion in the previous subsection, where only representations of
\mathfrak{su}(2)_{a}
with even Dynkin labels appear in the index. To determine whether there is a mixed anomaly involving the flavour symmetry, we fix
n_{a}=1/2
in (
3.4
); this amounts to turning on the second Stiefel-Whitney class
w_{2}^{a}
that obstructs the lift from the
\mathrm{SO}(3)_{a}
bundle to the
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{a}
bundle. The Dirac quantisation condition requires that the gauge fluxes
m_{1,2,3}
satisfy
\pm m_{1}\pm m_{2}\pm m_{3}\pm n_{a}\in\mathbb{Z}
, i.e.
\sum_{i}m_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}+\frac{1}{2}
. We tabulate the contributions of some of such gauge fluxes in certain examples below.

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.75}{
\begin{tabular}[]{|c|c|c|}\hline\cr CS levels&Gauge fluxes&Contribution to the index (\ref{indextwoT2})\\
&$(m_{1},m_{2},m_{3})$&\\
\hline\cr$(-2,3,6)$&$\left(\frac{1}{2},0,0\right)$&$X_{-2}\equiv\left(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2a^{2}}\right)x^{2}+\left(\frac{a^{2}}{2}+\frac{3}{2}+\frac{3}{2a^{2}}+\frac{1}{2a^{4}}\right)x^{5}+O(x^{7})$\\
&$\left(0,\frac{1}{2},0\right)$&$0$\\
&$\left(0,0,\frac{1}{2}\right)$&$X_{6}\equiv\left(\frac{a^{6}}{2}+\frac{a^{4}}{2}\right)x^{4}+\left(-\frac{a^{4}}{2}-\frac{a^{2}}{2}\right)x^{5}+\left(\frac{a^{4}}{2}+\frac{3a^{2}}{2}+1\right)x^{6}+O(x^{7})$\\
&$\left(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}\right)$&$0$\\
\hline\cr\hline\cr$(-4,6,12)$&$\left(\frac{1}{2},0,0\right)$&$X_{-4}\equiv\left(\frac{1}{2a^{2}}+\frac{1}{2a^{4}}\right)x^{3}+\left(-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2a^{2}}\right)x^{4}+\left(\frac{a^{2}}{2}+1+\frac{1}{2a^{2}}\right)x^{5}+O(x^{6})$\\
&$\left(0,\frac{1}{2},0\right)$&$X_{6}$\\
&$\left(0,0,\frac{1}{2}\right)$&$X_{12}\equiv\left(\frac{a^{12}}{2}+\frac{a^{10}}{2}\right)x^{7}+\left(-\frac{a^{10}}{2}-\frac{a^{8}}{2}\right)x^{8}+O(x^{9})$\\
&$\left(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}\right)$&$\left(\frac{a^{14}}{8}+\frac{a^{12}}{4}+\frac{a^{10}}{8}\right)x^{7}+\left(-\frac{a^{12}}{8}-\frac{a^{10}}{4}-\frac{a^{8}}{8}\right)x^{8}+O(x^{9})$\\
\hline\cr\hline\cr\end{tabular}}\end{split}

(B.3)

In contrast to the precedent example of one
T_{2}
building block, we see that only even powers of
a
appear in the above. We therefore do not observe a mixed anomaly between a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
one-form symmetry and the
\mathrm{SO}(3)_{a}
flavour symmetry.
